## PART 1 — THE ORDERED LIST

Ordered by consequence to a person accused of nothing: locational/safety first, then degrading and medical allegations, then identity surveillance, then scraped biography, then third parties other than Twiggs, then agenda and colour. **21 edits at 20 lines, grouped into 14 converged items.**

---

### TIER A — Locational and safety detail (can be acted on)

**1. §2.1 Timeline — apartment complex name.** *(three readers; unanimous in substance — one said "remove entirely" scoped to the parenthetical, two said "trim" scoped to the sentence; same operation)*

> `when Robinson moved in as a new roommate at the St. George apartment (Fossil Hills complex, per September 2025 reporting)`

**REMOVE ENTIRELY** — delete ` (Fossil Hills complex, per September 2025 reporting)`; the sentence keeps "the St. George apartment."

The cohabitation premise (§5.2 secondary transfer), the note under the keyboard, the Sept. 11 encounter and the "over 200 miles from Orem" point at §3.5 all work identically on "the shared St. George apartment," so the complex name bears on **no** public question while functioning as a residential address for a witness this same file records as having required a federal protective detail against threats.

---

**2. §9 Life Since September 2025 — current household.** *(three readers; unanimous, remove)*

> `A single low-verification aggregator adds that he lives "with his family" there; that detail is **not** in the court record and sits in unexamined tension with the widely reported family estrangement — treat as unverified.`

**REMOVE ENTIRELY** — delete the sentence; the preceding "Relocated to **Texas** — established in court records…" stays untouched.

The living-persons gate sits *earlier* than the confidence gate: correctly grading a Tier 4 detail does not license carrying it, and the only residency fact bearing on a public question is the **state**, which drives the §77-21-3 certificate, Graf's quashal and the Utah Supreme Court denial — and which is separately court-sourced in the same bullet.

---

**3. §6.1 Relocation and refusal of service — the same detail, mis-cited.** *(three readers; unanimous, trim)*

> `Twiggs **moved out of Utah to Texas**, where he reportedly lives with family (KUER; LAmag; court transcript).`

**TRIM TO ROLE AND RELEVANCE** → `Twiggs **moved out of Utah to Texas** (KUER; LAmag; Rev.com hearing transcript).`

The Texas relocation is the jurisdictional hinge of the entire §6 subpoena fight and must stay; the household clause bears on nothing **and** silently promotes a Tier 4 aggregator detail toward Tier 1 by bundling it with "court transcript," when §9 states in terms that it is *not* in the court record.

---

**4. §2.1 Timeline — dwelling floor plan.** *(two readers; both trim)*

> `Early reporting described a three-bedroom unit with a third roommate at some point (thenewsgrail.com).`

**TRIM TO ROLE AND RELEVANCE** → `An aggregator account adds that a third roommate was present at some point (thenewsgrail.com — unverified; bears, if true, on household access to the items at §5.2).` *(Deleting the sentence outright is the acceptable alternative.)*

The bedroom count is interior detail of a private person's home and bears on nothing, but household composition is **not** idle — the prosecution's secondary-transfer explanation turns on who could deposit DNA on the towel and screwdriver, and that question got sharper, not weaker, after the §5.1 correction established Twiggs' contribution was conditioned in rather than statistically supported.

### TIER B — Degrading, medical and private-conduct allegations

**5. §10 register — the National Pulse row.** *(three readers; two trim, one remove)*

> `| Drug use, "black-market HRT," living "in squalor" | The National Pulse (Oct. 31, 2025), anonymous/tabloid sourcing echoing one relative | **Unverified**; disputed by friends; no official corroboration |`

**TRIM TO ROLE AND RELEVANCE** → `| Uncorroborated tabloid allegations about his personal habits and living conditions | The National Pulse (Oct. 31, 2025), anonymous/tabloid sourcing echoing one relative | **Unverified**; disputed by friends; no official corroboration |`

Every other row in this table corrects a claim about the **case** (the surrender chain, Discord participation, the DNA majority, foreknowledge); this one corrects a claim about a private person's drug use, hormone sourcing and housekeeping, so the row's corrective function survives a non-enumerating description while grading a smear "unverified" does not stop the file republishing it verbatim. *Recorded dissent: the third reader would delete the row entirely, on the ground that no finding anywhere in the file depends on it — that becomes the right call if the trimmed row is ever found to be doing no work for a reader.*

**6. §12 Citations — the National Pulse URL.** *(one reader; paired with item 5)*

> `- The National Pulse (unverified tabloid claims, flagged): https://thenationalpulse.com/2025/10/31/report-tyler-robinsons-trans-partner-lance-twiggs-used-drugs-black-market-hrt-lived-in-squalor/`

This is the case where a citation is not a neutral pointer — the slug states `used-drugs-black-market-hrt-lived-in-squalor` in plaintext, so leaving it while trimming item 5 defeats item 5 for every reader who never follows the link; the source is cited nowhere else in the body, and outlet-plus-date preserves locatability.

> `A relative told Fox News Digital (Jan. 2026) that Twiggs "struggled with drug and alcohol abuse, was addicted to video games, and was grappling with his gender identity," and that these issues "fueled repeated conflicts with family members" (Fox News, Jan. 15, 2026).`

**TRIM TO ROLE AND RELEVANCE** → `A relative told Fox News Digital (Jan. 2026) that personal difficulties, including conflict over his gender identity, "fueled repeated conflicts with family members" (Fox News, Jan. 15, 2026 — tier 3, single unnamed relative).`

The estrangement contest is load-bearing — it is what the friends rebut, what Cox's faith remark engages, and what §1.3's "No court record addresses it" disposes of — but the *enumerated* conduct is unnamed-source allegation of private habits about a man accused of nothing, and the steelman fails on this record: the defence's actually-stated impeachment targets are the immunity agreements and "potential motives to cooperate" (§6.2), not his substance use. **Re-entry condition:** if a defence filing, testimony or ruling puts his substance use in issue, it returns at *that* tier, not this one.

*Adjudication note, because this is the sharpest split in the set:* one reader kept this passage entire on BOC-AUDIT 5.2 grounds — that removing hostile characterisation while keeping the friendly rebuttal is a one-directional audit trail. That objection is answered by applying items 8 and 9 in the same edit, not by keeping the enumeration.

**11. §1.2 Gender identity — the grandfather/black-sheep/speculation bullet.** *(three readers: one trim, two remove)*

> `- Twiggs' grandfather, asked by the Daily Mail in September 2025, declined to confirm whether Twiggs was transgender; another relative called Twiggs the "black sheep" of the family and said she "wouldn't be surprised" if he were transitioning (thenewsgrail.com; WION).`

**REMOVE ENTIRELY.**

The one reader who argued for a trim wanted to preserve the grandfather's declination as a genuine non-corroboration datum under the verify-in-both-directions rule — **and that datum is already carried, separately and better, at §8.1 line 221**, in the source catalogue where it belongs; with that established, nothing in the bullet survives the test: gender identity is confirmed at Tier 2 (Cox) and via the charging document and the note's "Luna" salutation, "black sheep" is a bare pejorative with no factual content, and "said she… wouldn't be surprised" both narrows an unidentified relative by gender and offers a family member's speculation about a private person's medical status.

### TIER E — Scraped biography asserted in the analyst layer

**15. §1.1 Established biographical facts — Education/occupation.** *(two readers; both remove)*

> `- **Education/occupation:** Reportedly a gifted pianist in school and a high academic achiever ("straight A" per friends quoted by the Times of India); a Facebook profile listed Utah Tech University; described himself online as a "digital creator" and aspiring professional gamer. No authoritative confirmation of degree completion exists in public records (Factually.co synthesis, Dec. 3, 2025).`

**REMOVE ENTIRELY** (the whole bullet, final sentence included).

No filing, ruling or testimony in this file turns on his piano ability, his grades, his university or his gaming ambitions, and the defect is compounded by placement — this sits under a heading reading "**Established biographical facts**," asserting scraped personal detail in the **analyst layer**, where the same underlying quotes are correctly handled as attributed recording-layer material at §8.2. The last sentence is worse than surplus: it commits the project to checking whether a private individual finished a degree and then records a null about it. Note also that "a Facebook profile listed Utah Tech University" is account-level identification of the subject himself, against a documented doxxing wave and a protective detail.

### TIER G — Research agenda and colour

**20. §11 Key Open Questions — item 4.** *(two readers: one remove, one keep-as-part-of-a-group)*

> `4. **The end and current character of the Robinson–Twiggs relationship** — undocumented beyond the "former partner" designation.`

**TRIM TO ROLE AND RELEVANCE** — delete item 4 and renumber; fold its only public-bearing residue into item 5, which becomes: `**Whether Twiggs will testify live at trial**, where confrontation rights attach fully; his credibility, any bias arising from the former relationship, his immunity terms, and the Sept. 12 interview contents (never played publicly) would all be tested for the first time.`

An open-questions list is a standing commitment to go find out, and as written this commits the project to establishing when and how a private individual's romance ended and what its "current character" is — nothing in the case turns on that answer, and the one thing that *could* (witness bias at trial) is item 5's territory and should be stated there; the companion sentence at §2.2 ("no public record establishes exactly when or how the relationship ended") is a defensible statement of an absence and **stays**. The reader who kept this scored items 3, 4 and 5 as a group without testing 4 separately.

---

**21. §7.1 The two interviews — personal description.** *(two readers: one trim, one considered-and-kept; DISCRETIONARY, lowest consequence in the set)*

> `Twiggs appears in the video in a suit and tie (NBC).`

**TRIM TO ROLE AND RELEVANCE** — delete the sentence, *or* keep it; applying this changes nothing material either way.

The load-bearing point a reader might draw from it — that the April 20 session was formal and sworn — is stated outright in the same bullet ("at the Utah County Attorney's Office," "Given **under oath**, in lieu of live preliminary-hearing testimony"), so the sentence adds only his person. **One correction to the keeping reader's ground:** they justified it as describing "a person in a public court proceeding," but the suit and tie were worn at a recorded interview in the County Attorney's Office, not in court — which weakens the keep without making the trim important.

## PART 2 — CONTESTED PASSAGES ADJUDICATED TO **KEEP**

These were flagged by at least one reader and are **not** removals. They are recorded here so that whoever applies Part 1 does not cut them by momentum.

**§1.1 — `Raised in what relatives and Gov. Cox described as a conservative, Latter-day Saint (Mormon) family.`** **KEEP.** One reader read this as converting relatives' faith and politics into asserted biography; but this is category (b) — a **named official's Tier 2 on-record statement**, and it is the pivot of the faith-conflict explanation of the estrangement that the friends advance and that §1.3 records as contested. The defect that reader identified is a *layer/attribution* question ("described" already attributes it), not a living-persons one, and it belongs to a different pass. *Separate open item, outside this pass: whether Cox's "very different than his family" was originally said of **Robinson's** family — a sourcing check, not a privacy one.*

**§2.2 — the Schmitt/Trump "trans girlfriend" claim.** **KEEP.** One reader wanted it relocated to the §1.2 analytical note; the material itself is squarely in project scope (media narratives about the case), attributed, graded as secondhand and unverifiable, and separately carried in the §10 register (line 256, verified present). The only real defect is **placement** — it sits under a heading reading "Official confirmations," where it is not one and borrows authority. **That is a structural fix, not a removal, and it is not counted below.**

**§8.1 — `Twiggs and family members received an **FBI security detail** in the weeks after the shooting due to threats.`** **KEEP.** One reader applied a security-disclosure test to the extension to family members; two kept it explicitly. It is historical (ended January 2026 per §8.3), the relatives are unnamed, it contains no address or itinerary, and it is the documented consequence of his public identification — the same doxxing wave the friends' "He doesn't deserve this" answers. The dissenting concern is better answered at §9 (item 17) than here.

**§1.3 — the friends' rebuttal (Times of India; Hindustan Times).** **KEEP**, with only the symmetric clause-level trim at item 9. Removing the derogatory half of a contested private-life question while keeping the flattering half — or the reverse — is itself a bias operation; both halves stay, in the trimmed non-enumerating form.

**§8.2 — the friends' character defence, including "straight-A student and gifted pianist."** **KEEP.** This is attributed recording-layer material from named outlets serving a named purpose, and it is the correct handling of the same underlying facts that §1.1 wrongly asserted in the analyst layer — which is precisely why item 15 removes §1.1's version and this one survives.

**§8.1 line 221 — the grandfather's declination.** **KEEP.** This is the non-corroboration datum that makes item 11 a clean removal rather than a trim; it must not be cut in the same pass, or the verify-in-both-directions record goes with it.

Also unanimously kept across all three readers, and untouched by Part 1: **§3.3** in full (Twiggs' own texts and the Q-135 correction restoring "I'm much more worried about you" to him; Robinson's rifle-provenance texts naming his father and grandfather); **§3.4** Discord's official correction and the unnamed friend; **§3.6** the surrender chain including the retired-deputy descriptor; **§4** in full; **§5.1–§5.2** in full, including the Q-135/Q-141 material and Bakker's percentages; **§6** in full including retained counsel and the refusal of service; **§1.1** name/pronoun paragraph and **§1.2** bullets 1–2 and the analytical note; **§7.3** identity/politics answers and the hunting-trip cover story; **§9** first bullet and the Distractify correction; **§10** rows on "tipped off police," Discord, the OANN inversion and foreknowledge; **§1.1** age and hometown; and every named official and professional throughout.

## PART 3 — THE COUNT, PLAINLY

**14 converged items, comprising 21 edits at 20 lines. Of the 21 edits, 8 are outright removals of a passage and 13 are trims.**

| Reading of "removals" | Count | Against seven |
|---|---|---|
| Passages deleted outright | **8** | **exceeded** by one |
| Distinct passages requiring living-persons action (removals + trims) | **21** | **exceeded** threefold |
| Distinct *facts* requiring action, collapsing the same detail in two or three places | **14** | **exceeded** — doubled |

**On every reading, seven is exceeded.** Nothing was manufactured to reach it; the honest observation is the opposite — the count passed seven without effort, and the pressure in this pass ran toward *restraint*, since six flagged passages were adjudicated back to KEEP.

**Two caveats on what "exceeded" can and cannot mean.** First, the original list is unrecoverable — `CR-REVIEW-2026-08-16G` §6 records that the working note was never written to disk and that git, the untracked and ignored trees, and the scratchpad were all searched — so this pass **cannot** be scored as a superset of it. Second, and for the same reason, it is entirely possible the original seven included one or more passages this pass deliberately **keeps** (the Fox News relative's account is the likeliest candidate: one of three readers kept it entire). "Exceeded" therefore describes what the re-derived pass found. It does not assert a containment relation to a list nobody can read.

The principal reason the number came out near double is structural, not editorial: **five of the fourteen items are the same fact standing in two or three places** — the "lives with family" detail at §6.1 and §9; the substance-use enumeration at §1.3, §8.1 and §8.2; the "black sheep" epithet at §1.2 and §8.1; Robinson's mother's tears at §3.4 and §7.3; the scraped biography at §1.1 and its citation at §12. A single-reading count would have counted each of those once.

## PART 4 — APPLICATION HAZARDS

**H1 — Concurrent editing.** The file is modified against HEAD and was written at 22:57:09 on 2026-08-16 by another session applying the Q-140 reconciliation (new case-posture header, §7.1 booking correction, §7.2 Tier 1 rewrite, two §10 rows resolved, §11 item 6 rewritten). All 21 passages were re-verified verbatim in that post-edit state and none had been touched, but **line numbers will move again**. Anchor on the quotes. Coordinate before writing, or you will take a lost update.

**H2 — Paired edits; applying one half is worse than applying neither.** Six pairs must move together: **11+12** (or the epithet survives), **7+8+9** (or the §1.3 trim is cosmetic, and the rebuttal loses its object), **5+6** (or the URL slug reproduces verbatim the words the row trim removed), **15+16** (or a dangling citation preserves the framing), **2+3** (or the file contradicts itself about whether the detail is in the court record), **18+19**. This file already exhibits exactly this failure twice — v1.1 log item 3 applied at §3.7 and left standing at §7.1, and v1.1 log item 4 applied at §9 and left standing at §6.1. **Do not make it three.**

**H3 — Orphaned revision-log rows.** Item 13 orphans **v1.1 log item 6** ("platform of the 'Lance'→'Luna' display-name change softened"), which will point at a passage that no longer exists. Item 2 orphans **v1.1 log item 4** (the §9 "living with family" downgrade). Under the corrections-in-place rule, both need superseding rows at the v1.3 end saying the passage was removed and why — **not** silent edits to the v1.1 rows, which are the historical record of what was decided on 2026-07-10.

**H4 — Dangling citations after the removals.** **Verified by grep:** removing items 13 and 15 leaves the **Factually.co** citation (line 334) supporting nothing in the body except, at most, the uncited "born ca. 2002–2003" inference at §1.1 — re-anchor it there or drop it. **thenewsgrail.com** survives at lines 58 and 234 and its citation stays. **WION** survives at line 67 (Schmitt) and its citation stays, but its parenthetical descriptor "(grandfather; Schmitt claim; …)" goes stale once item 11 is applied and should be trimmed to "(Schmitt claim; early identification caveats)". **Times of India**, **Hindustan Times** and **Daily Mail** references all survive elsewhere; their citations are unaffected.

**H5 — Register integrity.** No D-, V-, F- or M-number resolves on any passage in Part 1, and no §10 row points at one except the National Pulse row itself (item 5, trimmed not deleted). The §10 "trans girlfriend" row at line 256 is verified present and is the correct home for the Part 2 §2.2 placement note.

## PART 5 — RESIDUAL SCOPE

This pass tested **living-persons bearing only**. It did **not** re-verify any tier marking, any citation's accuracy, any URL's liveness, or any factual claim in the file — and it did not run the completeness pass, which `CR-REVIEW-2026-08-16G` §7 records as still open under Q-140 ("verifying what a file says cannot find what it never said"). The header's own warning that every tier marking in v1.2 is provisional remains in force and is untouched by anything above.

Two observations fall outside this pass's lens but were surfaced by it and should be routed, not dropped: **(a)** whether Cox's "very different than his family" was originally said of Robinson's family rather than Twiggs' — an attribution question; **(b)** the §2.2 Schmitt placement under an "Official confirmations" heading — a structural question. Neither is a living-persons removal and neither is counted above.

## PART 2 — Completeness findings that survived both adversarial lenses


### S1. [day2-dna] The towel was first read as a THREE-person mixture; Twiggs' elimination sample is what collapsed it to two — so the third, unknown contributor was resolved into him

- **Direction:** MORE — it credits Mr. Twiggs' profile with explaining minor peaks the examiner had assigned to an unknown third person, and simultaneously makes the headline towel statistic more contingent on him than the profile states anywhere
- **Confidence (finder):** High
- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/prelim-hearing-transcripts/day2.txt (Bakker, examined by Burt; speakers inferred from content — Burt asks, Bakker answers and refers to "my" case file)`
- **Profile passage:** ABSENT — the profile never says this. Patterns run against the profile that WOULD have matched: "number of contribut" (0 hits), "NoC" (0 hits in the DNA sense), "third contributor"/"third individual" (0), "electropherogram" (0), "allele" (0), "unknown" (0). "three" returns only "three-bedroom unit", "three-way conflict", "about three minutes"; "contributor" returns only the §5.1/§10/§13 lines about the likelihood ratio and the 5%/95% and 11%/89% splits. The nearest passage, §5.1, says only: "Mixed male DNA from two individuals was reported on (a) the dark towel ... and (b) the screwdriver" — it treats two-ness as given.

**Source quote (verbatim):**

> Michael Burt: "And your original assessment was that there were three contributors to this sample, right?" — Ms. Bakker: "That is correct, yes." ... "I changed my conclusion on September 13th of 2025." ... "I was submitted an elimination sample from Mr. Twiggs. ... When I compared him to this sample, all of the DNA, unlike that major contributor, matched to Mr. Twiggs. So because of that, I determined that there's actually only two individuals, because those small peaks, there was nothing left for a third individual to be compared to."

**Why it matters:**

This is the item where a third person's DNA appears, and the profile has no idea it exists. On 12 September 2025 the examiner read the towel (item 7) as a three-person mixture, reasoning from sub-threshold peaks that "the 12 belongs to one individual and those two peaks that are not called belong to a second or third individual"; her technical reviewer (Tara Benson) had independently called it TWO and then agreed to three after discussion. On 13 September, Twiggs' buccal standard arrived and she reversed to two. So Mr. Twiggs' profile did not merely get added to an existing two-person model — it absorbed peaks that had for a day been attributed to an unidentified third person. That matters twice over. (1) The number of contributors is an INPUT the analyst supplies to STRmix, and she conceded changing it changes the answer (Burt: "But it does change it, correct?" — Bakker: "Yes."). The 1.7-octillion figure for the towel is therefore computed on a two-person model that exists only because Twiggs was conditioned in; the profile's §5.1 correction argues the statistic never TESTED him, but misses that the statistic's own architecture DEPENDS on him. (2) The profile's §5.2 "what the record does not contain" list, and §11 open question 3, are both written as though the only unexplained thing is how Twiggs' minority DNA got onto the items — when the record also contains a live, briefly-held expert reading in which the minor material on the towel belonged to someone nobody has identified.

*Corrected/narrower form:*

§5.1 of the profile omits the number-of-contributors history on the towel swab (item 7.1), and should carry it — stated at Tier 1 from day2.txt (the examiner's answers on cross; speakers inferred from content per standing hazard, never from Rev labels):

(a) The number of contributors is an input the analyst supplies to STRmix — "That's my determination based on my experience and training looking at the data. That's something I input into STRmix."

(b) On or about 12 September 2025 the examiner interpreted item 7.1 as originating from three contributors, reasoning from unlabelled sub-threshold peaks that a third individual "could" be present; her technical reviewer (Tara Benson) had drawn her own conclusion of two before agreeing to three after a "quick discussion."

(c) On 13 September 2025, on receipt of Mr. Twiggs' voluntarily provided elimination standard, she revised the interpretation to two, because the non-major DNA matched him and "there was nothing left for a third individual to be compared to."

(d) The two-person interpretation underlying the reported 1.7-octillion towel figure therefore postdates, and was reached in light of, Mr. Twiggs' standard — which is a fact about the model's history, not about him.

Two limits the entry must state, both from the same witness:
- NO THIRD PERSON WAS EVER DETECTED. NoC=3 was a one-day estimate about what unlabelled sub-threshold peaks "could" mean, and the examiner testified "we never truly know the number of contributors in a forensic sample." The record does not support saying an unidentified third person's DNA was found on the towel and then reassigned to Mr. Twiggs; it supports only that the basis for the three-contributor possibility disappeared once his standard was compared.
- THE TWO-PERSON READING IS NOT ATTRIBUTABLE TO HIM ALONE. The technical reviewer read two from the same electropherogram before his standard existed. And the concession that changing NoC "does change" the likelihood ratio came with the qualification that where the person compared is the major contributor (Robinson, 95% on the towel), moving from three to two "doesn't change the likelihood ratio of that major contributor by much" — STRmix re-runs varying within a magnitude of 10 in any event. Quote the "Yes" only with that qualifier attached.

Correct the rationale too: §5.1 ALREADY states that both headline statistics carry Mr. Twiggs in both competing propositions. What is missing is (i) the NoC history above and (ii) that NoC is an analyst-supplied input — not the conditioning point, which the file already makes. §5.2's "what the record does not contain" and §11 open question 3 should be amended only to note that the three-contributor possibility on the towel was closed by comparison to Mr. Twiggs' standard rather than by further testing — not to assert an unexplained third person.


**Verifier 2 — refuted=False**

The completeness claim holds. The profile nowhere contains the contributor-count history, in any form. Patterns capable of matching it all return zero: `three|3` within 40 characters of `contribut` (0), `number of contrib` (0), word-bounded `NoC` (0), `technical review|peer review|reviewer|Benson` (0), `mixtur|deconvol` (0), `unknown|unidentif` (0), `allele|electropher|locus|loci` (0). The named capable pattern is `(three|3)\D{0,40}contribut|contribut\D{0,40}(three|3)`, which would match "there were three contributors" or "three-person mixture" and does not fire. Every non-zero hit was opened, not counted: the 10 `contribut` hits are all §5.1/§10/§13 likelihood-ratio and 5%/95%, 11%/89% lines; the 2 `third` hits are "three-bedroom unit with a third roommate" (L58) and "third-party/reliability strategy" (L162); the 3 `noc` hits are "innocence"/"innocent"; the 7 Sept-12/13 hits are all the interview date, none DNA. The profile is at the repo root, not the CR-ACQ folder, so the U+FFFE hazard does not apply to it.

The nearest passage, §5.1 (L150), does treat two-ness as given in its superseded text ("Mixed male DNA from two individuals was reported on..."), exactly as the finding says. The closest the file comes is L151's "case notes of 15 September 2025... 'Report will still be issued with Twiggs as an assumed contributor per previous discussions'" — but I read that note's full transcript context (07:04:20), and "previous discussions" refers to discussions with Agents Fulger and Carmack about using Twiggs as an assumed contributor, not to the three-to-two count change. It does not gesture at the NoC history.

The source carries every element, verified in day2.txt via artifact_search.find() under BOTH the join and hyphen readings (speakers inferred from content: Burt asks, Bakker answers and refers to "my" case file, "my technical reviewer"). Burt/Bakker at 06:12:28-35 (original assessment of three contributors on sample seven); "approved by Tara Benson" at 06:21:07, with Bakker explaining the reviewer "would have called it a number of contributor of two" and "agreed with me calling it three"; the September 12 reasoning at 06:32:02; "I changed my conclusion on September 13th of 2025" at 06:33:30; the elimination-sample reversal and "nothing left for a third individual" at 06:33:52. Item 7 is confirmed as the towel twice (Faumuina 05:08:38, "Towel from around Mauser or rifle"; Bakker on redirect 08:07:18). The 1.7-octillion figure is item 7.1, phrased with Twiggs in both propositions. I also found corroboration the finder did not cite: at 07:10:02 Burt walks the September 15 STRmix run for item 7-1 and Bakker agrees the summary of input tells the software "Assume there are two contributors."

Two overstatements require narrowing, both in the direction of claiming less. (1) The "third, unknown contributor" was never an established person. Bakker's September 12 reasoning was expressly conditional on uncalled sub-threshold peaks — "it COULD BE that the 12 belongs to one individual and those two peaks that are not called belong to a second OR third individual" — an NoC estimate, not an identification of a third person's material. Her stated basis for revising was a match, not a substitution: "all of the DNA, unlike that major contributor, matched to Mr. Twiggs." So "the third, unknown contributor was resolved into him" and "the minor material on the towel belonged to someone nobody has identified" assert an entity the record never established, and about a man accused of nothing. (2) The finding leans on "But it does change it, correct?" - "Yes." (06:10:37) while dropping the answer it responded to: Bakker had just testified that where the major contributor is the one being compared, moving NoC from three to two "doesn't change the likelihood ratio of that major contributor by much." That is a concession the number moves, not that it moves materially, and it will not carry "the statistic's own architecture DEPENDS on him." Project rule 6 (steelman before resolving) requires the qualifier travel with the quote.


*Corrected/narrower form:*

The profile omits the contributor-count history of the towel (item 7), and should record it in the analyst layer at Tier 1 as follows.

On or about 12 September 2025 the FBI examiner (Bakker) estimated the number of contributors (NoC) to item 7.1 — the swabbing from the towel around the Mauser 98 — as three. She reached three by reading peaks BELOW the 150 RFU analytical threshold, and her reasoning was explicitly conditional: "it could be that the 12 belongs to one individual and those two peaks that are not called belong to a second or third individual" (day2.txt 06:32:02). No third person was ever identified, and none was ever affirmatively established — three was an NoC estimate drawn from ambiguous sub-threshold peaks, not a finding that a third person's material was present. Her technical reviewer, Tara Benson, independently notated two, discussed it with her, and agreed to three (06:17:54, 06:20:27, stamp at 06:21:07).

On 13 September 2025 she revised the count to two (06:33:30). The stated reason was Mr. Twiggs' elimination sample: "When I compared him to this sample, all of the DNA, unlike that major contributor, matched to Mr. Twiggs. So because of that, I determined that there's actually only two individuals, because those small peaks, there was nothing left for a third individual to be compared to" (06:33:52). The narrow, supported statement is therefore: the sub-threshold peaks that had supported a three-contributor hypothesis were ACCOUNTED FOR by Mr. Twiggs' profile once it was available for comparison — not that an established unknown third contributor was reassigned to him.

Two consequences follow, both stated at their supported strength. First, NoC is an input the analyst supplies to STRmix, not an output ("NoC stands for Number of Contributors"; "That's my determination based on my experience and training", 05:59:16-30), and the 15 September STRmix run on item 7-1 that produced the 1.7-octillion figure was set up on a two-contributor assumption ("you tell the software program, 'Assume there are two contributors,' right?" - "Correct, yes.", 07:10:02). So that headline statistic rests on a two-person model arrived at by comparing Mr. Twiggs, and it already carries him in both of its competing propositions ("if Twiggs and T. Robinson are contributors than if Twiggs and an unknown, unrelated person are contributors") — which is the point §5.1 already makes. What must NOT be added is that the figure materially depends on the count: Bakker testified the opposite, that where the major contributor is the one being compared, changing NoC from three to two "doesn't change the likelihood ratio of that major contributor by much," conceding only that "it does change it" (06:09:51-06:10:38). Any use of the "it does change it" concession must carry that qualifier.

Second, §5.2's "what the record does not contain" list and §11 open question 3 should be widened: the unexplained item is not only how Mr. Twiggs' minority DNA reached the towel and screwdriver, but also that the count of contributors to the towel was itself revised — held at three for roughly a day, against a reviewer's initial reading of two — and that the revision turned on his elimination sample. This bears on the reliability of the NoC determination, which the defence cross-examined at length; it is not evidence of any third participant, and it implies nothing adverse about Mr. Twiggs, who is accused of nothing.


### S2. [day2-dna] On the screwdriver — the rooftop item — three of Twiggs' alleles are affirmatively ABSENT from the evidence sample, the profile is only partial, and no Twiggs-alone statistic was ever run on that item at all

- **Direction:** LESS — the transcript supports materially less about Mr. Twiggs' presence on the rooftop screwdriver than the profile's "~11% contributor" and "I did see his DNA present" convey

- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/prelim-hearing-transcripts/day2.txt (Bakker; speakers inferred from content — questioner vs. witness answering from her own electropherograms)`
- **Profile passage:** §5.1: "on the screwdriver the split was **Robinson ~89% / Twiggs ~11%**"; and §5.1's steelman: "*\"I visually looked at the DNA sample… he also had those peaks\"*; *\"When I compared him to that data, I did see his DNA present.\"*" — The profile carries the 11% figure and the "I did see his DNA present" basis without ever recording that on this item specifically the comparison produced three non-matches. Patterns run: "allele" (0), "loci"/"locus" (0), "D16" (0), "D19" (0), "THO" (0 in the DNA sense), "partial" (returns only the burned note and the v1.2 header), "item" (returns only "household items", "two items", "both items").

**Source quote (verbatim):**

> Burt: "At D16, Mr. Twiggs was a 912, right?" — Bakker: "That is correct, yes." — Burt: "And did you find 912 at that location in the 8-1 sample?" — Bakker: "The only piece of information I had for sample eight at item D16 was a 12." ... "At D19S, what was Mr. Twiggs' type?" — "It was a 1416." — "Did you find a 16 allele in the 8A sample?" — "No, I did not." ... "At THO, what was Mr. Twiggs' type?" — "He was a 79.3." — "Did you find a seven allele in the 8.1 sample?" — "No, I did not. But again, this is a mixture and there is some dropout." And: "Item eight was a partial profile, so I did not develop DNA at every single location."

**Why it matters:**

The screwdriver is the item that puts Mr. Twiggs' DNA at the alleged firing position — the profile itself calls it the defence's "wedge for reasonable doubt" and lists it as open question 3. On that item the record is materially weaker as to him than the profile's "~11%" implies: at three of the tested loci his known types were not observed in the evidence sample, and the examiner's answer was not that they were there but that she would not exclude him for it — at THO1 expressly "when comparing an elimination, I would essentially ignore that location." Item 8 also yielded only a partial profile: "For item eight, I did not have a full profile, so I did not have data at all 23 locations to compare it to either Mr. Robinson or Mr. Twiggs." Corollary, on her own testimony rather than on a search null: the single Twiggs-alone STRmix run in evidence (Baker 38, pp. 39–44) is captioned "dated September 15th and it relates to item 7-1" — the towel — and asked what further statistics were run she said "I ran statistics with just Twiggs comparing that individual to the sample… I think that was the only additional." So the LR of 1 the profile reports is a towel figure; on the SCREWDRIVER no statistic of any kind was ever calculated on Mr. Twiggs. The profile's sentence "the only likelihood ratio ever calculated on Mr. Twiggs alone was 1" is true but reads as though both items were tested and both came back uninformative; one item was tested, the other was not tested at all.

Yes."). Item 7 = towel (L4797, L9359); item 8 = screwdriver (L4821, L9367). So the LR of 1 is a TOWEL figure. Confirmed.
- The profile's completeness gap is real: lance-twiggs-comprehensive-profile.md contains no "allele", "loci", "D16", "D19", "THO", "dropout", or the item-8 partial profile, and §5.1 never identifies which item the LR-of-1 run was on.

Faumuina reading the FBI report at the State's prompting - "The DNA results from Item 8, sub 1 are 30 quintillion times more likely if Twiggs and T. Robinson are contributors, than if Twiggs and an unknown, unrelated person are contributors"; (2) Bakker's own item-8 mixture proportion, L6559 "For item eight, one of the contributors was 11% and the other was approximately 89%", and on redirect L9355 "Twiggs better aligned with that 11% contributor" (the trailing "79%" there is a Rev garble for 89%). The ~11% figure the finding faults the profile for carrying IS itself an item-8 statistic about him. WHAT IS OVERSTATED — "three of Twiggs' alleles are affirmatively ABSENT" asserts what the witness expressly declined to say. At D16 Bakker would not adopt Burt's "was not a 912", reframing it as "That was the only piece of information that was recovered at that location"; at THO she volunteered "there is some dropout... does not mean I would automatically exclude someone". In a degraded partial mixture, non-detection is not affirmative absence — which is the whole of her answer. Also "at three of the tested loci his known types were not observed" is imprecise: at D16 one of his two alleles (the 12) WAS observed, and at D19S and THO counsel asked about only ONE allele each (the 16, the 7), so the transcript never establishes whether his 14 and 9.3 were found.

MINOR: the finding says the exhibit is "captioned" as relating to item 7-1; that caption text is not in the transcript — counsel described it and the witness affirmed. And her "that was the only additional" is hedged ("I believe", "I think"), so the no-Twiggs-alone-run-on-item-8 conclusion rests on hedged testimony plus the single admitted exhibit, not on a documentary null.


*Corrected/narrower form:*

On item 8 (the screwdriver from the rooftop perch), the FBI examiner testified on cross-examination that the evidence sample yielded only a PARTIAL profile — "Item eight was a partial profile, so I did not develop DNA at every single location"; "For item eight, I did not have a full profile, so I did not have data at all 23 locations to compare it to either Mr. Robinson or Mr. Twiggs" (Bakker, day2.txt L6055, L6087; T1) — and that at three loci put to her, one of Mr. Twiggs' two known alleles each was NOT DETECTED in that sample: at D16 his 9 (only a 12 was recovered), at D19S his 16, and at TH01 his 7 (L8158-8258; T1). She did not treat these as absences establishing anything: she attributed non-detection to dropout in a degraded mixture ("just because I didn't see a seven, for example, does not mean I would automatically exclude someone"), and at TH01 said that where only one individual is represented, "when comparing an elimination, I would essentially ignore that location." The transcript does not establish whether his other allele at D19S and TH01 was found. Non-detection here is NOT affirmative absence and NOT an exclusion, and must not be written as either.

On the statistics, stated precisely: the single Twiggs-alone STRmix run in evidence (Baker 38, pp. 39-44) was on item 7-1, the TOWEL — the witness confirmed both counsel's description of the exhibit ("That is correct, yes") and the evidence input ("You input sample 7A?" - "Correct. Yes.") (L7793-7805; T1) — and she testified, hedged, that comparing "just Twiggs" was "the only additional" statistic run (L7629; T1). So on her own hedged testimony no Twiggs-ALONE likelihood ratio was ever calculated on the screwdriver, and none is in evidence.

But statistics concerning Mr. Twiggs WERE calculated on item 8: the reported LR of 30 quintillion (read into the record from the FBI report by Sgt. Faumuina, L4913; T1) and the mixture proportion of approximately 11% / 89% with Twiggs aligned to the 11% contributor (Bakker, L6559 and L9355; T1). Neither TESTS his presence — the LR carries him as a conditioned contributor in BOTH competing propositions, and the mixture proportion is a model output downstream of that same conditioning. The correct statement is therefore: on the screwdriver Mr. Twiggs was never the subject of a statistic capable of discriminating as to him — not that no statistic was run.

The completeness gap in §5.1 that this supports is: the profile carries the ~11% figure and the examiner's visual-inspection basis without recording (a) that item 8 yielded only a partial profile, (b) that at three loci one of his alleles each was not detected, or (c) that the LR-of-1 run it cites was on the towel, not the screwdriver. Confidence: High for (a)-(c) as gaps and for every quoted item above (direct reading of T1 transcript). Residual scope: I did not re-verify the towel-side loci, the underlying FBI report or STRmix printouts (not held), or any day other than day2.

"leading" is the pattern capable of matching "leading examination". The nearest thing the profile has is §7.1's parenthetical: "the court later ruled McBride's own statements in the recording were hearsay and directed focus onto Twiggs' answers" — which stops well short and does not say the objection was overruled.)

**Source quote (verbatim):**

> this is actually the Ryan McBride 1102. The vast majority of this video is Mr. McBride testifying and Mr. Twiggs agreeing. If we were in a courtroom, it would be a leading examination virtually from start to finish. … [the court:] The court agrees. It is hearsay. At the same time, I'm only going to consider Mr. McBride's statements to provide context for the things that Lance Twiggs says and for their effects on Lance Twiggs.

**Why it matters:**

The profile's own framing note stakes everything on this recording: "Everything attributed to 'what Twiggs has said' comes exclusively from his two recorded law enforcement interviews." §3.1, §3.5 and §7.3 then present a list of things "Twiggs stated" — met Robinson in 2023, Robinson left at 4–5 a.m., the tearful Sept. 11 encounter, never heard Robinson mention Kirk, the Dremel request. The Tier 1 record contains an officer-of-the-court characterization, made in open court against the exhibit and never rebutted by the State when it replied, that the form of most of the recording is the prosecutor asserting and Mr. Twiggs agreeing. The magistrate partly credited it — he found McBride's statements hearsay and admitted them only "to provide context … and for their effects on Lance Twiggs" — and separately confirmed the content from the bench ("much of this is the text messages"). The profile records the substance of the recording without recording its form, and form is what decides whether a given line is Mr. Twiggs' account or his assent to someone else's. For a file about a private individual accused of nothing, attributing a prosecutor's narration to him is the same class of error as the Q-135 misattribution already corrected in §3.3. Speaker basis: the characterization is argument addressed to "Your Honor" invoking "our client's right to a fair trial" against a State exhibit — content identifies defence counsel; the ruling is first-person judicial disposition of an objection — content identifies the magistrate. Rev's labels were not relied on.

A. The profile really does omit it. 'narrat' hits 4 times, all about media/charging narratives, never about the recording's form. The capable pattern is 'leading', which would match 'leading examination'; it hits once in the whole file, at line 178, inside 'rulings would deprive Robinson of compulsory process' — unrelated. 'context' and 'effect' each hit exactly once and are 'second-appeal context' (line 342, a URL annotation) and 'effective assistance' (line 178) — so even the limited-purpose half of the finding is absent in those terms. The profile is not in the CR-ACQ folder and contains zero U+FFFE, so the both-readings hazard does not apply to it; I nonetheless confirmed day3.txt contains zero U+FFFE and re-ran the source searches under BOTH readings via artifact_search.norm(join=True) and norm(join=False) — 'leading', 'counsel testifying' and 'effects on Lance Twiggs' each returned 1 under both. The only adjacent text is §7.1 line 190's parenthetical ('the court later ruled McBride's own statements in the recording were hearsay and directed focus onto Twiggs' answers'), which records the hearsay finding but not the form characterization, not the overruling, and not the terms of the limitation.

B. The source carries the quote. day3.txt line 2172 (defence argument) and line 2384 (the ruling) are verbatim as quoted. Speaker inference is sound and independent of Rev's labels: line 2172 is argument addressed to 'Your Honor' invoking 'our client's right to a fair trial' against a State exhibit, and at line 2230 the same speaker enumerates 'it's counsel testifying... it's hearsay... best evidence rule... 403' — content identifies defence counsel; line 2384 is first-person disposition of an objection ('The court agrees... is overruled') — content identifies the magistrate. I also confirmed the exhibit's identity rather than assuming it: State's Exhibit 16 is the April 20, 2026 recorded 1102 statement (lines 2088–2124, immediately following testimony that the April 20 interview was recorded and 'taken in lieu of him having to come and testify at the preliminary hearing').

C. Four overstatements against direct reading.
1. 'is on the record as a leading examination' asserts the record ESTABLISHES the form. It does not. This is one party's characterization in argument resisting the exhibit, framed counterfactually — 'IF WE WERE IN A COURTROOM, it would be a leading examination' — and expressly self-hedged in the very next sentence: 'I don't want to say that every single statement by Mr. Twiggs is just an affirmation of what Mr. McBride says, but the vast majority of it is.' The finding drops both the counterfactual and the hedge.
2. 'the court admitted the prosecutor's half only for context and effect' misstates the disposition. The court did close to the opposite of a limited admission: it OVERRULED the hearsay objection and admitted the exhibit whole — 'The Court is considering the entirety of State's Exhibit 16' (line 2640) and 'That has been admitted in its entirety for the Court's determination as it relates to this case' (line 2656). What was limited was the PURPOSE for which the court would CONSIDER McBride's statements, not the scope of admission. 'Not for its truth' is the analyst's gloss; the court never said it, though context and effect-on-listener are not-for-truth purposes, so the substance is fair.
3. 'The magistrate partly credited it' overreaches. The magistrate credited the HEARSAY characterization of McBride's statements. He never addressed, adopted or ruled on the leading/form characterization anywhere in day3.txt. And 'much of this is the text messages' (line 2772) was said inside the Rule 403 cumulative-evidence analysis; it corroborates the separate best-evidence point (McBride reading exhibits back), not the leading-examination point.
4. 'never rebutted by the State when it replied' is true but needs its scope stated. The prosecutor replied procedurally and EXPRESSLY RESERVED substantive argument, asking the court to decide open-vs-closed first because she wanted to 'quote specific sections' (line 2268), and again at line 2352 ('I want to reserve the right to make my substantive arguments using what's actually in the exhibit'). She never engaged the point on the open record before or after the ruling. But the public transcript has an unexplained gap between recess at 01:42 and resumption at 02:41, and the court later authorized a closed hearing limited to timestamps 32:55–35:48 (line 2452). So the null is a null about the OPEN record, not about the proceeding.

D. On the Twiggs-protective test. The finder's underlying caution is legitimate and is the same class of error §3.3's Q-135 correction fixed: §3.1/§3.5/§7.3 present items as things 'Twiggs stated' without recording that the recording's form was contested. But the finding as written slides toward asserting as established that Mr. Twiggs was mostly ASSENTING rather than recounting — a characterization advanced by counsel adverse to the exhibit and never adjudicated. Adopting it would claim more about a man accused of nothing than the record carries — in the opposite direction, but by the same mechanism, as the error it warns against. The fix is to record the characterization AS a characterization, in the recording layer, and to state the ruling accurately.


*Corrected/narrower form:*

The profile omits the contested FORM of the April 20 recording (State's Exhibit 16) and should add it in the recording layer, attributed and not adopted:

RECORDING LAYER (T1, day3.txt read directly; speakers inferred from content per standing hazard 9, never from Rev's labels). On Day 3, arguing against admission and publication of State's Exhibit 16, defence counsel characterized the recording's form: 'this is actually the Ryan McBride 1102. The vast majority of this video is Mr. McBride testifying and Mr. Twiggs agreeing. If we were in a courtroom, it would be a leading examination virtually from start to finish' — immediately hedging, 'I don't want to say that every single statement by Mr. Twiggs is just an affirmation of what Mr. McBride says, but the vast majority of it is.' The framing is COUNTERFACTUAL ('if we were in a courtroom'). Pressed by the court to name the evidentiary category, counsel gave four — 'it's counsel testifying,' hearsay, best evidence, and Rule 403 — and sharpened the form point to its narrowest version: 'There are many portions of it which are not the statement of Mr. Twiggs. They're Mr. McBride testifying on video as to what other exhibits say. And Mr. Twiggs is saying, "Yeah, that's what it says."'

ANALYST LAYER. (i) This is one party's characterization in argument, made while resisting the exhibit. It is NOT a judicial finding and NOT evidence. The magistrate never adopted, endorsed or ruled on the leading/form point anywhere in the day3 record. (ii) The State did not answer it on the open record: the prosecutor replied procedurally and expressly reserved substantive argument pending a ruling on closure so she could quote the exhibit, and never returned to it. That null is scoped to the OPEN record — the public transcript has an unexplained gap between the 01:42 recess and the 02:41 resumption, and the court separately authorized a closed hearing limited to timestamps 32:55–35:48. (iii) On the hearsay objection the magistrate ruled: 'The court agrees. It is hearsay. At the same time, I'm only going to consider Mr. McBride's statements to provide context for the things that Lance Twiggs says and for their effects on Lance Twiggs. As such, the hearsay objection ... is OVERRULED.' So the objection was overruled and the exhibit admitted IN ITS ENTIRETY — 'The Court is considering the entirety of State's Exhibit 16'; 'That has been admitted in its entirety for the Court's determination.' What the court limited was the PURPOSE for which it would consider the prosecutor's statements (context, and effect on the listener — non-hearsay purposes), not the scope of admission. (iv) Partial independent corroboration of the narrower best-evidence version, not of 'leading': in its Rule 403 cumulative-evidence analysis the court observed of the redacted stretch, 'to throw back the curtain, much of this is the text messages, to be clear.'

CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS FILE, stated narrowly. Because the form of the recording was contested and never adjudicated, and because the court itself distinguished the prosecutor's statements from Mr. Twiggs' for purposes of consideration, §3.1, §3.5 and §7.3 should mark that items introduced as things 'Twiggs stated' derive from a recording whose question-and-answer form one party characterized as largely assent — and that this project has NOT independently verified, line by line, which items are Mr. Twiggs' own account and which are his agreement to a proposition put to him. Confidence: HIGH that the characterization was made, went unrebutted on the open record, and was never adjudicated; UNVERIFIED as to whether any specific §7.3 item is assent rather than account, since this library holds neither the recording nor its transcript. What would change it: Exhibit 16 itself or the 21-page transcript referenced on the record, which would let each attributed item be graded individually.

### S6. [day4-interview] Twiggs' own sworn account places him at his parents' house on 11 September 2025 — the profile states no court record addresses the estrangement question

- **Direction:** LESS — it narrows a hostile, relative-sourced characterisation the profile currently records as unadjudicated and untouched by any court record
- **Confidence (finder):** High
- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/prelim-hearing-transcripts/day4.txt`
- **Profile passage:** **Status:** The estrangement itself is consistently reported across friendly and hostile sources. The *cause* (substance abuse vs. faith/identity conflict) is contested, maps directly onto who is speaking (relatives vs. friends), and is unadjudicated. No court record addresses it.

**Source quote (verbatim):**

> Lance Twiggs (State's Exhibit 16, played day4): "so I just went back to my parents' house."

**Why it matters:**

A court record does address it — a sworn one, in evidence, in his own words, and the profile's flat "No court record addresses it" is therefore false as of the day it describes. So NONE of §11's factual items can be reached on 09-01: not who burned the note (1), not the note's tense discrepancy (2), not the screwdriver or the mechanism of Mr. Twiggs' DNA (3), not whether he testifies live (5), not the redacted interview contents (6). The only output available is the probable-cause/bind-over determination — and even that is not promised: nothing on the record says Judge Graf will rule from the bench that day, so the profile should not imply he will. Compounding it, the State spent Day 5 arguing the magistrate is legally BARRED from the weighing those questions would require — quoting State v. Hester, "The magistrate may not sift or weigh the evidence, but must leave those tasks to the fact finder at trial," alongside Schmidt, Pledger, Virgin, Droesbeke and Lopez. §11's frame needs to move from "pending, 09-01 may reach some" to "evidentiarily closed; these are trial questions or nothing." Also new and absent: the four-hour cap (profile: "four hours" = 0 hits), the in-person requirement, Robinson's requested presence, the defence resting ("rebutt" = 0 hits), and Robinson's election not to testify. SPEAKER BASIS: the continuation and adjournment language is a scheduling ruling followed by the bailiff's "All rise"; "no further witnesses" answers the court's "Anything further from defense?"; "no rebuttal evidence" answers "Returning to prosecution. Anything further?" — inferred from content, not labels.

*Corrected/narrower form:*

The profile already states, in three places, that 2026-09-01 is the hearing's last day "given over to argument" (posture banner, §11.6, §13). What it omits is that the EVIDENTIARY RECORD IS ALREADY CLOSED, and the hearing's mechanics. On Day 5 (2026-07-10) the defence rested — "No, we have no further witnesses, Your Honor" (answering the court's "Anything further from defense?"); the State expressly waived rebuttal — "The state has no rebuttal evidence to present at this hearing" (answering "Returning to prosecution. Anything further?"); and defence counsel made a record that Mr. Robinson, so advised, elected not to testify, the court noting "he's nodding in the affirmative." The court then continued the preliminary hearing to September 1, 2026 at 10:00 AM "for an in - person hearing," allocating "up to four hours total for argument. That's encompassing both parties," with the defence requesting Mr. Robinson's physical presence. [T1, day5.txt lines 2580–2648; speakers inferred from content — question-vs-answer and scheduling-ruling-followed-by-"All rise" — not from Rev labels. High confidence.] CONSEQUENCE, stated narrowly: because no further witness or exhibit will be received, 09-01 cannot produce NEW FACTS, and so cannot answer the factual open questions in §11 that depend on testimony — who burned the note (11.1), the note's tense discrepancy (11.2), the screwdriver's purpose and the mechanism by which any Twiggs-attributed DNA would have arrived on it (11.3, and note §5.1: his presence was assumed, not established), or whether Mr. Twiggs testifies live, which is a trial question (11.5). It does NOT follow that 09-01 can reach nothing: §11.6 concerns publication of the redacted April 20 material, which is a sealing/publication ruling rather than evidence (see §7.2: "Publication only — not exclusion from the court's consideration"), and on Day 5 Judge Graf held that "if motions are filed, the court will entertain motions in brief argument ahead of the September 1st hearing and address it at that time." Nor is a bind-over decision promised for that day: nothing on the Day 5 record states when Judge Graf will rule ("bind", "bound", "advisement", "written ruling" all return zero across both U+FFFE readings of day5.txt), so the profile should not imply a same-day determination in either direction. Accordingly §11.6 should read, in substance: "The evidentiary record closed on 2026-07-10. 2026-09-01 is four hours of in-person argument on a closed record, with Mr. Robinson's presence requested; the factual questions above are therefore trial questions or unanswerable on this record, though the court may still rule on publication and other motions that day, and the timing of any probable-cause determination is not stated on the record." Separately, and at lower weight: on Day 5 the State (Chad Grunander, ~01:06–01:11) placed on the record — as the stated authority for a prospective standing objection to the scope of defence witness testimony, not as sustained argument — the line of Utah authority that the magistrate must view evidence most favourably to the prosecution and may not weigh it, citing State v. Schmidt, State v. Pledger, State v. Hester ("The magistrate may not sift or weigh the evidence, but must leave those tasks to the fact finder at trial"), State v. Virgin, State v. Droesbeke and State v. Lopez. Mr. Twiggs is accused of nothing and none of this bears on him personally; Mr. Robinson has not been bound over and is presumed innocent.


### S9. [information-and-pc] §3.3 mis-orders the closing sequence of the T1 exchange, and never records that the State pleaded the two witness-tampering instructions on two different dates

- **Direction:** MORE — the State pleaded Robinson's instructions to Mr. Twiggs as spanning two days, and pleaded the final one as a reply to a message Mr. Twiggs himself sent.
- **Confidence (finder):** High
- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/2025-09-16_information-charging-document_dc27880532.txt`
- **Profile passage:** Robinson's closing instructions: **"Delete this exchange"**; don't talk to media; don't take interviews; "If police ask questions, ask for a lawyer and stay silent"; "I'm going to turn myself in willingly"; "You are all I worry about love"

**Source quote (verbatim):**

> Robinson: Im gonna turn myself in willingly … Robinson: you are all I worry about love / Roommate: I'm much more worried about you / Robinson: don't talk to the media please. don't take any interviews or make any comments. … if any police ask you questions ask for a lawyer and stay silent

**Why it matters:**

The profile's order is reversed at the exact passage it corrected on 2026-08-16 (Q-135). In the Information the sequence is: "delete this exchange" → two intervening Robinson messages about his father → "Im gonna turn myself in willingly" → "you are all I worry about love" → Mr. Twiggs' reply → and only then, as the LAST message reproduced, "don't talk to the media please … ask for a lawyer and stay silent". The profile puts the media/lawyer instruction third and Twiggs' reply nowhere in the list. So even after Q-135 restored the reply's authorship, a reader still cannot see that the final instruction — the one the profile calls the factual basis of a witness-tampering count — is Robinson's answer to Mr. Twiggs saying he was worried about him. Compounding this: the Information pleads "COUNT 5: TAMPERING WITH A WITNESS … on or about September 10, 2025" (the delete instruction) and "COUNT 6: TAMPERING WITH A WITNESS … on or about September 11, 2025" (the stay-silent instruction). The State charged the exchange as spanning two calendar days. §3.3 presents it as one continuous conversation "After reading the note", following §3.1's "about 11 p.m." on 10 September, and §3.5 then treats 11 September as the in-person day. grep -i "Count 5" / "Count 6" over the profile: zero hits under either casing; "witness tampering" hits only line 107, which names the counts but gives neither their dates nor their contents. Steelman noted: "on or about" is elastic and an exchange running past midnight would explain the split without any second-day contact — but the profile records neither the dates nor that reading.


**Verifier 1 — refuted=False**

Both halves survive direct reading of the Information, and the ordering half is independently corroborated by a second T1 source. (1) ORDER. The Information reproduces the tail of the exchange as: "Robinson: delete this exchange" -> "Robinson: my dad wants photos of the rifle ... Hes calling me rn, not answering." -> "Robinson: since trump got into office [my dad] has been pretty diehard maga." -> "Robinson: Im gonna turn myself in willingly, one of my neighbors here is a deputy for the sheriff." -> "Robinson: you are all I worry about love" -> "Roommate: I'm much more worried about you" -> "Robinson: don't talk to the media please. don't take any interviews or make any comments. ... if any police ask you questions ask for a lawyer and stay silent", which is the LAST message reproduced before the "Search of Robinson's Residence" heading. The profile's line 105 lists the media/interviews/lawyer material at list positions 2, 3 and 4 and puts "I'm going to turn myself in willingly" and "You are all I worry about love" at 5 and 6 - inverted relative to the source. Speaker attribution is not at risk here: this is a court filing, not a Rev transcript, and content confirms the labels independently (the media/lawyer text is an instruction, and Count 6's "to wit" clause and the Count 6 recap paragraph both attribute "if any police ask you questions ask for a lawyer and stay silent" to Robinson; "I'm much more worried about you" is a reciprocal expression of worry answering "you are all I worry about love"). CROSS-CHECK: the defence's 2026-08-11 brief, quoting State's Exhibit 16.1 at pp. 27-28, gives the same order - "You are all I worry about love ... Don't talk to any media please. don't take any interviews or make comments. ... I will always love you too... ... If any police ask you questions ask for a lawyer and stay silent..." So the profile's order is contradicted by both primary sources it relies on, not one. (2) DATES. Verified at T1 under BOTH the join and hyphen readings via artifact_search.find(): "COUNT 5: TAMPERING WITH A WITNESS ... in that on or about September 10, 2025 ... directing his roommate to delete incriminating text messages" and "COUNT 6: TAMPERING WITH A WITNESS ... in that on or about September 11, 2025 ... directing his roommate to stay silent if police questioned the roommate." The profile records neither date and never ties either count to its instruction. Nulls run with patterns capable of matching the thing said absent: grep -iE "count ?5|count ?6|count5|count6" over the profile returns zero (the file's other "count" hits are "charge count", "account", "counted", "counter"); grep -i "on or about" returns zero; grep -iE "sept(ember|\.)? ?11|9/11" returns only the Sec 3.5 heading and the CBS date flag, both about the in-person encounter, not the counts. The "tamper" stem hits lines 89 and 107 only; both name the counts, neither gives a date or a content-to-count mapping. The framing consequence the finder alleges is also textually present: Sec 3.1 anchors first contact at "about 11 p.m." on 10 September, Sec 3.3 opens "After reading the note, per the filings:" as one continuous turn-ordered exchange, and Sec 3.5 then treats 11 September as the in-person day - so nothing in the profile lets a reader see that the State pleaded the last instruction as occurring on or about a different calendar day from the first.


*Corrected/narrower form:*

SURVIVES, with five narrowings - all of which claim less, and three of which run in Mr. Twiggs' favour.

(a) "Twiggs' reply nowhere in the list" overstates. The reply IS recorded in Sec 3.3, correctly attributed, in the Q-135 correction annotation appended to the same line 105: it renders the Information as "Robinson: 'you are all I worry about love' / Roommate: 'I'm much more worried about you'." What is missing is that the annotation stops there - it never shows that the media/lawyer instruction FOLLOWS that reply - while the bullet list itself places that instruction before both. Supported form: the reply is absent from the instruction list and the annotation does not extend far enough to restore the order, so a reader still cannot recover the fact that the stay-silent instruction is Robinson's answer to the roommate's expression of worry.

(b) "puts the media/lawyer instruction third" is imprecise. The profile splits the material into three list items at positions 2, 3 and 4 ("don't talk to media"; "don't take interviews"; "If police ask questions, ask for a lawyer and stay silent"). The splitting is defensible - the defence brief's fuller StEx 16.1 quotation shows the media instruction and the lawyer/silence instruction are separate messages with "I will always love you too. I will write to you as often as possible." between them, occupying the Information's internal ellipsis. The defect is placement, not division: all three sit before "turn myself in willingly", "you are all I worry about love", and the roommate's reply, when the source puts all three after.

(c) The Information pleads against "his roommate" and never names Mr. Twiggs, in Counts 5 and 6 or in the reproduced exchange. The finding should say "the Roommate's reply as the Information renders it"; the identification of that roommate as Mr. Twiggs rests on other record sources (SBI Agent Davis's testimony, official statements, and the defence brief, which does name him). CR-REVIEW-2026-08-16G line 60 already makes this same point.

(d) "two intervening Robinson messages about his father" is true of the Information AS REPRODUCED, not of the underlying thread. The Information's rendering carries ellipses on both sides of the block, and the defence brief's StEx 16.1 quotation shows further messages the Information omitted entirely ("leave the room alone. The feds will search it top to bottom..."; the roommate's question "is there any way you don't go to prison?"; "If you need a place to stay im sure my family would have you in my old room"). State the ordering claim as "the order in which the State reproduced the exchange", which is the yardstick Sec 3.3's own caption ("as reproduced in charging documents") sets.

(e) The date defect is a recording gap, not evidence of a second day of contact. The corrective is to record what the State pleaded - Count 5 "on or about September 10, 2025" (delete the exchange), Count 6 "on or about September 11, 2025" (ask for a lawyer and stay silent) - together with the steelman the finder correctly noted: "on or about" is elastic and an exchange running past midnight would explain the split without any second-day contact. Nothing in the Information, and nothing found in the profile, establishes a second-day contact, and the profile must not be edited to imply one.

Also worth carrying into any fix, though outside this finding: the profile's rendering of the Count 6 message is materially shorter than the record. StEx 16.1, per the defence brief, continues "...ask for a lawyer and stay silent. My lawyer should be Doug Terry, I'll see if he can reach out to you. And make sure the police follow the right procedures, proper warrants and stuff, but be polite and respectful of course." That remainder is the whole of the defence's Count Six "purely altruistic advice" argument, and Sec 3.3 - which at line 107 calls these instructions the factual basis of the two tampering counts - currently gives the reader neither it nor the surrounding order.

Two small corrections to the finding's own evidence: "witness tampering" also appears hyphenated at line 89 ("witness-tampering counts"), so the claim that it hits only line 107 is wrong as written - immaterial, since line 89 likewise supplies no date and no count-to-content mapping. And the artifacts-folder hazard was checked: the Information contains exactly one U+FFFE (in "low-crawling", nowhere near the passages at issue), and every pattern above returned identical hits under both the join and hyphen readings.

The profile places that final message third in a semicolon list whose other bullets track the exchange in order. Mr. Twiggs' reply is not absent from 3.3 — the Q-135 correction note in the same bullet reproduces it as a two-person pair — but nothing in 3.3 shows that Robinson's media/lawyer instruction immediately FOLLOWS that reply. Say "immediately follows", not "is Robinson's answer to": the Information reproduces the messages sequentially, without timestamps and with ellipsis markers showing elided material, so it establishes adjacency in the reproduction, not responsiveness.

(b) DATES. The profile nowhere records that the Information pleads the two witness-tampering counts to two different dates: COUNT 5 "on or about September 10, 2025" (delete the incriminating text messages) and COUNT 6 "on or about September 11, 2025" (stay silent if police questioned the roommate). Profile line 107 names "the two witness tampering counts" without their dates or their respective contents; "on or about", "Count 5", "Count 6", "76-8-508" and "third degree" return zero hits file-wide. State the pleading, not a conclusion about the conversation: the State pleaded the two acts to two different "on or about" dates — it did not plead, and the Information's probable-cause narrative does not say, that the exchange spanned two calendar days. "On or about" is elastic and an exchange running past midnight would explain the split without any second-day contact; the profile records neither the pleaded dates nor that reading.

Nothing here bears on Mr. Twiggs' conduct or status: he is accused of nothing, the counts are Robinson's, and the only fact about Mr. Twiggs involved is that he sent one message saying he was worried about Robinson.

(1) "The WHOLE instruction sequence as 'purely altruistic'." False to the brief. The brief never mentions Count Five and never uses the word "delete": searched "Count Five" (0), "Count 5" (0), "delet" (0, stem). Per the Information, Count 5 is the tampering count for "directing his roommate to delete incriminating text messages" and Count 6 for the stay-silent advice. Writing the argument as covering the whole sequence would give the defence a broader position than it took.

(2) "An instruction addressed to Mr. Twiggs about THAT EXACT ROOM… which bears directly on the burned-note question." The brief never identifies whose room or which room. The same excerpt names a different room two sentences later — "my old room" at Robinson's family's home — and the brief elides on both sides ("…"), so the message's place in the sequence and its timing are not established by this source. This matters in the direction the standing rule protects: §11 Q1 is the profile's suspicion-adjacent question, and an inference is being run from an undated, unattributed "the room" to the burned note. Note also that on the finder's own reading the instruction is to leave a room UNDISTURBED, which if anything cuts away from any suggestion that Mr. Twiggs handled the note further — but that inference is equally unsupported and should not be written either.

Nothing establishes any offer was made or accepted; Doug Terry is not among counsel of record on this brief (Nester, Novak, Burt, Visser). Record the words, not a transaction, about a named third party.

Minor: "moving to strike Count Six" reads as a standalone motion. It is a bind-over argument inside the 2026-08-11 preliminary-hearing briefing that the State failed to establish probable cause.

### S12. [disqualification-and-officials] On the day he charged the case, the charging prosecutor refused four times to exculpate the roommate — the profile's §8.3 entry for that very press conference records none of it, and §4 says the opposite

- **Direction:** LESS — the primary record is markedly less exculpatory than the profile's "consistently said they do not believe he participated," and §8.3's two-line Gray entry omits the entire adverse half of what Gray said
- **Confidence (finder):** High
- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/2026-08-16_rev_update-on-charlie-kirk-shooter.txt`
