**Source quote (verbatim):**

> Q: "could we see charges against the roommate?" — Gray: "Again, I'm not prepared to answer that question." … Q: "are you confident that the roommate had no part of this attack whatsoever?" — Gray: "Again, I'm not going to comment on the evidence."

**Why it matters:**

§8.3 catalogues this press conference and extracts exactly two things from it, both favourable-to-neutral. Read directly, Gray declined FOUR times on the roommate: whether charges could follow ("not prepared to answer"); whether he was confident the roommate had no part ("not going to comment on the evidence"); whether the evidence so far showed any involvement (same); and whether "his parents and the roommates" were still cooperating ("I'm not going to comment on that either"). This is Tier 2 read directly, and it directly undercuts §4's line — "Authorities have consistently said they do not believe he participated in planning or executing the shooting" — whose only citations are NYT-via-Daily-Wire (July 2026) and Cox's "had no idea." The one official with charging authority pointedly did not say it, on the record, on 2025-09-16. ABSENT — the profile never records any of these four exchanges; I searched "declin" (matches at lines 40, 42, 171, 221, 233 — so the pattern is demonstrably capable of matching a rendering of Gray declining, and does so on the transgender-motive question only), plus "no part" (0), "confiden" (0), "charges against" (1 hit, the closing boilerplate about Robinson). The fix is NOT to imply suspicion — a prosecutor's refusal to characterise evidence is orthodox — but §4's word "consistently" is not carried by the record and should be narrowed, with Gray's non-answers recorded in the recording layer. Speaker basis: content, not the Rev label — the speaker enumerates the counts he is filing, names his own trial team, and says "I am filing a criminal information"; the questioners self-identify ("Carter Evans from CBS News", BBC).


**Verifier 1 — refuted=False**

SURVIVES ON THE QUOTES, REFUTED ON THE CHARACTERISATION — so: not refuted, but materially overstated.

WHAT VERIFIED. I read the cited file in full (392 lines; U+FFFE count 0, so the artifact-folder hyphen/line-break hazard does not apply, and I did not rely on a search null over it). All four quoted exchanges exist and are ANSWERS, not questions, and not counsel's characterisation: (1) BBC questioner, "The governor previously said the roommate is cooperating, but could we see charges against the roommate?" → "Again, I'm not prepared to answer that question." (lines 199→203). (2) Carter Evans, "are you confident that the roommate had no part of this attack whatsoever?" → "Again, I'm not going to comment on the evidence." (331→335). (3) Carter Evans, "But the evidence so far, does it show any involvement?" → "Again, I'm not going to comment on other than the facts or the evidence that we've gathered so far." (339→343). (4) Carter Evans, "Are his parents and the roommates still cooperating?" → "I'm not going to comment on that either." (355→359).

SPEAKER BASIS (content, not Rev label). The monologue speaker says "Today after reviewing the evidence… I am filing a criminal information charging Tyler James Robinson," enumerates Counts 1–7, files the death-penalty notice "independently as county attorney," and introduces "my trial team… Chad Grunander, who is one of my two chief deputies" — only the county attorney can say these. In the Q&A the turns are structurally question-then-answer; questioners self-identify (Carter Evans/CBS, Ed Lavandera/CNN, "Dennis… with the BBC", Ben Winslow/Fox 13 News) and address "Mr. Gray"; the answerer speaks in the charging authority's first person ("as attorneys, we typically like to control that information"). The moderator's own opening states "Jeff is going to be the only one speaking right now." The known label unreliability is visible in this file — "Speaker 2" covers two different questioners (165, 181) and the question at 231 is orphaned by the next label — but it does not disturb these four Q/A pairs.

OMISSION FROM THE PROFILE VERIFIED. ~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/lance-twiggs-comprehensive-profile.md contains no U+FFFE. Capability-satisfying pattern: "declin" returns 8 hits (lines 40, 42, 171, 179, 221, 233, 243, 365) — it demonstrably matches renderings of an official declining, including §8.3's own Gray entry at 233 and §1.2's transgender-motive decline at 42 — and it matches NONE of the four roommate exchanges. "not prepared", "prepared to answer", "not going to comment", "comment on the evidence", "no part", "confiden", "additional suspect" each return 0. §8.3 (line 233) does derive from this press conference and extracts exactly two items, both favourable-to-neutral. A genuine tier point the finder understated: the profile's only knowledge of this presser is The Advocate (line 42) — i.e. T2 via T3 — while the library now holds the Rev transcript and can read the presser at T2 directly.

WHY THE FINDING IS OVERSTATED — TWO SOURCE-BASED REFUTATIONS.
(a) "Refused four times… pointedly did not say it" is not carried by the transcript. Gray gave a non-answer to 19 of roughly 24 questions, including ones with no roommate content whatsoever: whether the texts were a confession (219), whether Robinson is cooperating (227, 263), whether Robinson expressed remorse (255), whether Robinson had a lawyer (287), what radicalised him (247), whether he practised (375), the timing of the texts (155), the transgender-motive question (171), lone-wolf (187), and whether he consulted Erika Kirk (391). He stated the policy on the record twice — "Why are we reluctant to share the details of the investigation itself and comment on the case? Because I want to ensure a fair and impartial trial" (107) and "as attorneys, we typically like to control that information, to preserve an impartial jury and a fair trial" (271). Four roommate non-answers inside a blanket, expressly-reasoned no-comment posture are not a pointed refusal to exculpate, and the finder's own caveat that the refusal is "orthodox" is contradicted by its own headline and by "pointedly did not say it."
(b) The finding omits a countervailing Gray answer in the same Q&A that runs the other way. Ben Winslow: "are you planning to file additional charges against anyone else in connection with this?" → Gray, substantively: "Again, we don't have any information at this point of additional suspects, but I know that our law enforcement agencies are continuing to follow leads" (line 179). That is the charging prosecutor, on the record, on the charging day, saying there were no additional suspects then. It is weaker and more time-limited than §4's sentence and it does not name the roommate — and it sits alongside "They haven't ruled that out" on advance knowledge (147) — but a completeness finding that reads this transcript for what it lacks and does not record what it contains has itself failed the completeness test it is applying.
(c) Item (4) is miscategorised. "Are his parents and the roommates still cooperating?" is a question about cooperation STATUS, not about involvement or exculpation. It bears on §8.3's Cox-derived "very cooperative", not on §4's participation sentence. Three of the four, not four, are on the involvement/charges point.
(d) On §4: a non-answer does not create an inconsistency among things officials actually said, so "consistently" is not falsified. What IS supported is narrower — §4 generalises to "Authorities" from two named sources (NYT-via-Daily-Wire, itself flagged elsewhere in the file at line 258 with an access-path caveat, plus Cox), while §4 line 138 already carries the more careful Mason formulation. The charging prosecutor, asked directly, neither joined nor contradicted that statement.

LIVING-PERSON NOTE. Nothing here bears adversely on Mr. Twiggs. He is not named anywhere in the transcript — Gray says "the roommate" throughout — and he is accused of nothing. Recording these non-answers is a recording-layer act about what a prosecutor declined to characterise; it must not be written in a way that reads as an analyst-layer implication about him.


*Corrected/narrower form:*

NARROWER SUPPORTED FORM (recording-layer addition + one attribution narrowing; no adverse inference about Mr. Twiggs):

1. §8.3 UNDER-EXTRACTS THE 2025-09-16 PRESS CONFERENCE, AND RESTS ON THE WRONG TIER. The profile's only account of this press conference is §1.2 line 42, cited to The Advocate — T2 via T3. The library now holds the Rev transcript (2026-08-16_rev_update-on-charlie-kirk-shooter.txt), so the presser is readable at T2 directly. §8.3's Gray entry should be re-sourced to the transcript and expanded.

2. WHAT TO ADD, IN THE RECORDING LAYER, TO §8.3. At the 2025-09-16 charging press conference Utah County Attorney Jeff Gray declined to characterise the evidence as to Robinson's roommate on three points — whether charges against the roommate could follow ("Again, I'm not prepared to answer that question"), whether he was confident the roommate "had no part of this attack whatsoever" ("Again, I'm not going to comment on the evidence"), and whether the evidence so far showed any involvement ("Again, I'm not going to comment on other than the facts or the evidence that we've gathered so far") — and separately declined to confirm whether Robinson's parents and roommates were still cooperating ("I'm not going to comment on that either"). Speaker established by content, not the Rev label.

3. THE CONTEXT THAT MUST TRAVEL WITH IT, OR THE ENTRY MISLEADS. These were four of roughly nineteen non-answers across about twenty-four questions. Gray also declined to say whether the texts were a confession, whether Robinson was cooperating, whether Robinson had shown remorse, whether Robinson had counsel, what radicalised him, and whether he had consulted Erika Kirk — and stated the reason on the record twice, including "as attorneys, we typically like to control that information, to preserve an impartial jury and a fair trial." A prosecutor's refusal to characterise evidence pre-trial is orthodox; nothing in these exchanges is pointed, roommate-specific, or evidence of suspicion. Drop "refused… to exculpate" and "pointedly did not say it" entirely.

4. RECORD THE COUNTERVAILING ANSWER FROM THE SAME Q&A (verify-in-both-directions). Asked by Ben Winslow whether he planned to file additional charges against anyone else, Gray answered substantively: "Again, we don't have any information at this point of additional suspects, but I know that our law enforcement agencies are continuing to follow leads." Grade it for what it is — not roommate-specific, expressly time-limited ("at this point"), and paired with "They haven't ruled that out" on whether anyone had advance knowledge — but it is an on-record statement from the charging prosecutor running toward, not against, §4's proposition.

5. THE §4 EDIT IS AN ATTRIBUTION NARROWING, NOT A REVERSAL. Replace "Authorities have consistently said they do not believe he participated in planning or executing the shooting" with a named-source form, e.g. "Gov. Cox said the partner 'had no idea that this was happening' (T2); investigators' view of non-participation is attributed to the New York Times as accessed via Daily Wire (T3, access-path caveat — primary NYT text not directly reviewed); DPS Commissioner Mason said on 2025-09-15 that authorities believed Robinson acted alone while investigating whether anyone had foreknowledge." Add, at most: the charging prosecutor, asked directly on 2025-09-16, declined to characterise the evidence either way. §4's existing sentence "No subsequent official finding has implicated Twiggs" stands and is unaffected.

6. STANDING QUALIFIER. Mr. Twiggs is not named in the transcript — Gray says "the roommate" throughout — and he is charged with no crime. Confidence in the underlying quotations: High (T2, transcript read directly, full file). Confidence that the omission matters: Medium — it is a real completeness gap and a real tier upgrade, but it changes the sourcing of §4's sentence, not its truth. Residual scope: I did not re-verify §8.3's Cox or Bongino entries, the NYT-via-Daily-Wire item, or the other three 2026-08-16 Rev files.


**Verifier 2 — refuted=False**

SURVIVES ON ABSENCE, OVERSTATED IN CHARACTERISATION AND IN ITS READING OF §4.

ABSENCE — CONFIRMED with capable patterns (profile read in full, 381 lines; no U+FFFE/U+FEFF/replacement chars present, so the artifact-folder dual-reading hazard does not bite this file, and I checked). The single strongest capable pattern is "comment": three of the four Gray answers contain that word verbatim ("I'm not going to comment on the evidence" / "not going to comment on other than the facts or the evidence that we've gathered so far" / "not going to comment on that either"), so any verbatim or near-verbatim rendering must hit it — it returns ZERO across the whole profile. "prepared" returns exactly one hit, the footer "Prepared for ongoing research", so exchange (a)'s "I'm not prepared to answer that question" is absent too. "declin" returns 8 lines (40, 42, 171, 179, 221, 233, 243, 365 — the finder listed only five of them), is demonstrably capable of rendering Gray declining at lines 42 and 233, and does so on the transgender-motive question ONLY. Also zero: "confiden", "no part", "exculpat", "exonerat", "no comment", "would not say", "stopped short", "additional", "under investigation"; "charges against" hits once and it is the closing boilerplate about Robinson; the four "lone" hits are all "alone" in the DNA section. Repo-wide grep over *.md finds no file recording "not prepared to answer", "additional suspects", "no part of this", "comment on the evidence" or "confident that the roommate", and the banked reconciliation CR-REVIEW-2026-08-16G does not mention Gray or the press conference at all. So the recording-layer gap is real and unbanked.

SOURCE — the four exchanges exist as quoted (lines 199-203, 331-335, 339-343, 355-359 of the Rev file). Speaker basis is content, not label, and it holds: the speaker is introduced by the county communications manager as "county attorney, Jeff Gray", says "I am filing a criminal information charging Tyler James Robinson", enumerates Counts 1-7, and introduces "my trial team ... Chad Grunander, who is one of my two chief deputies"; the Q&A alternates self-identifying questioners against answer-form turns in prosecutorial voice ("the evidence that we've gathered so far").

WHERE IT OVERSTATES — three ways, one of them decisive.

(1) DECISIVE: the finding is built one-directionally from the declines and omits Gray's own answer at the SAME Q&A that runs the other way. About 24 lines before the BBC question, Ben Winslow (Fox 13) asked "are you planning to file additional charges against anyone else in connection with this?" and Gray answered: "Again, we don't have any information at this point of additional suspects, but I know that our law enforcement agencies are continuing to follow leads." So "the one official with charging authority pointedly did not say it, on the record, on 2025-09-16" is NOT carried by the source. He said something dated and limited, but he said it. Presenting only the four refusals is exactly the audit trail that only ever weakens one side which Objectivity Mechanic 1 forbids.

(2) "§4 says the opposite" is wrong twice. A prosecutor's no-comment is not a contrary statement — it contradicts nothing. And the finder read §4's first bullet in isolation: the VERY NEXT bullet of §4 already records Utah DPS Commissioner Beau Mason (Sept. 15, 2025) that "authorities believed Robinson acted alone but were investigating whether anyone had foreknowledge." §4 is therefore not an unqualified exculpation; it already carries the open-question qualifier one line below the sentence the finding attacks.

(3) "Refused FOUR times to exculpate the roommate" over-characterises the set. Only (b) — "are you confident that the roommate had no part of this attack whatsoever?" — is squarely a refusal to exculpate. (a) is about charging discretion, not culpability. (c)'s answer is not the "(same)" the finder writes: it is "I'm not going to comment on other than the facts or the evidence that we've gathered so far", i.e. he will speak only to what he already presented — narrower than a flat refusal. (d) asks whether "his parents and the roommates" are STILL COOPERATING — an update on cooperation status, bundled with Robinson's parents and phrased in the plural; declining to update on cooperation is not a refusal to exculpate.

LIVING PERSONS — the headline construction "refused four times to exculpate the roommate" reads as suspicion of a man accused of nothing, notwithstanding the finder's own disclaimer. The correction must run toward claiming less, and toward recording both halves of what Gray actually said.

MINOR — "§8.3 catalogues this press conference" is loose: §8.3 is a general officials catalogue, and of its two Gray items only "declined to endorse identity-based motive framing" is press-conference-sourced (via The Advocate, per §1.2); "built the charging narrative around the texts" traces to the Information. The entry is not labelled to that event at all.

RESIDUAL SCOPE — I did not re-verify §4's NYT-via-Daily-Wire citation or Cox's "had no idea" against their primary sources; I did not check the other three 2026-08-16 Rev files for further Gray remarks; and I assessed only the Twiggs profile, not the other person-files.


*Corrected/narrower form:*

NARROWER SUPPORTED FORM — recording-layer omission only; no §4 contradiction.

The profile records nothing from the Q&A portion of the 2025-09-16 charging press conference except the single transgender-motive decline (§1.2 line 42, cited to The Advocate; echoed in §8.3 line 233). §8.3's Jeff Gray entry — the profile's only officials-catalogue entry for the prosecutor with charging authority — extracts two favourable-to-neutral items and nothing else from that day.

What is ABSENT and belongs in the recording layer (Tier 2, Rev transcript read directly, speaker inferred from content per standing hazard 9), stated in BOTH directions because the source runs both ways:

(i) Asked whether charges against the roommate could follow, Gray said: "Again, I'm not prepared to answer that question."
(ii) Asked whether he was "confident that the roommate had no part of this attack whatsoever," Gray said: "Again, I'm not going to comment on the evidence."
(iii) Asked whether "the evidence so far" showed "any involvement," Gray said: "Again, I'm not going to comment on other than the facts or the evidence that we've gathered so far."
(iv) Asked whether "his parents and the roommates" were still cooperating, Gray said: "I'm not going to comment on that either." (Cooperation status, bundled with Robinson's parents — not a culpability question.)
(v) AND, at the same Q&A and BEFORE (i), asked whether he planned "to file additional charges against anyone else in connection with this," Gray said: "Again, we don't have any information at this point of additional suspects, but I know that our law enforcement agencies are continuing to follow leads."

Items (i)-(iv) and item (v) go in together or neither goes in. Recording (i)-(iv) alone would import the finding's own one-directional error into the file.

CHARACTERISATION — do NOT write "refused four times to exculpate." Write the orthodox description: a prosecutor at a charging press conference declining to characterise evidence about an uncharged witness, which is standard practice and carries no adverse inference. Only exchange (ii) is squarely a declined exculpation.

§4 — NO correction on the "says the opposite" theory; a no-comment contradicts nothing, and §4 already carries the qualifier at its Mason (Sept. 15, 2025) bullet ("investigating whether anyone had foreknowledge"). The most that is warranted is an optional citation broadening: the "Authorities have consistently said..." sentence currently rests on NYT-via-Daily-Wire (July 2026) and Cox, and Gray's dated "no information at this point of additional suspects" (2025-09-16) may be added as a third, expressly time-bounded data point — which strengthens rather than narrows the sentence. If "consistently" is nonetheless thought thin, narrow it on the ground that its cited support is two sources, not because Gray said anything to the contrary.

Confidence: High that the four declines and item (v) are absent from the profile (capable patterns "comment", "prepared", "declin", "confiden", "no part" all checked, and "comment" — capable of matching three of the four answers verbatim — returns zero). High that the quoted exchanges are in the source as rendered. Grade the omission a recording-layer completeness gap, not a finding against §4.

## PART 3 — Split verdicts (one lens refuted, one did not) — NOT confirmed, queued


### P1. [information-and-pc] The affidavit's T1 account of Mr. Twiggs' first police interview is entirely absent — including his own characterisation of the Discord material as "a joke" and the fact that investigators photographed his screen message-by-message rather than receiving his phone contents

- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/2025-09-16_affidavit-of-probable-cause_dc27880530.txt`

**Source quote:**

> Investigators interviewed the roommate, who stated that his roommate (referring to Robinson) made a joke on discord. Investigators asked if he would show them the messages on discord, he opened it and showed several messages to investigators and allowed investigators to take photos of the screen as each message was shown by Robinson's roommate.

**Why it matters:**

This is the only Tier 1 record of what Mr. Twiggs actually said and did at his first contact with investigators, and the profile has none of it. Three separate gaps: (1) The word "joke" appears nowhere in the profile — grep -i "joke" over the profile returns zero, and grep -i "jok" returns exactly one hit, Twiggs' own text "You're joking, right????" (line 95), so a pattern capable of matching "made a joke on discord" was run and found nothing. His first-reported framing of the Discord material to police was that Robinson had made a joke — which sits directly against §3.4, where the Discord thread is sourced to Discord Inc.'s corporate statement and CNN/AP (Tier 3) and never to this affidavit. (2) The mechanism claimed by the profile is broader than the record: the affidavit describes a mediated, message-by-message screen-photo session in which the roommate controlled what was displayed ("as each message was shown by Robinson's roommate") — not a handover of phone contents and not a forensic extraction. The profile's §3.7 sentence carries no citation at all. "phone contents" and "handed over" appear only at line 129; "photos of the screen" is absent (grep -i "screen" hits only the §7.2 redaction passage). (3) Timing: this affidavit is signed "/S/ Davis, Brian 09/12/2025 04:00" and already contains the roommate interview, so the first interview was complete before 4 a.m. on 12 September — within hours of Robinson's evening surrender. §3.7 dates it only to "Sept. 12, 2025" on AP's authority (Tier 3) and expressly declines to anchor it; the affidavit anchors it at Tier 1. grep -i "04:00", "4 a.m", "overnight" over the profile: all zero.


**Verifier 1 — refuted=True**

The quote is verbatim in the affidavit (lines 114–118 of 2025-09-16_affidavit-of-probable-cause_dc27880530.txt), but three of the finding's four propositions fail on direct reading of the primary record.

(a) "The only Tier 1 record" — false. Day 4 of the preliminary hearing covers the first interview in far greater detail than the affidavit. The turns are unmistakably Q-and-A (counsel asks "How do you know that?", "How did law enforcement get this photograph?"; a law-enforcement witness answers in first-person narrative about chain of custody and says "No, I was not there" about the interview itself) — so I am reading these as witness answers on content, not on the Rev label. The witness testifies: the first interview was "the morning of September 12th, 2025" at "St. George PD," with "the two FBI agents," "audio/video recorded"; "physically, he shows them the phone and then allows them to photograph the phone"; the photos were taken by "Agent Larson with FBI" and became State's Exhibits 16.1 (text thread) and 16.2 (Discord); Twiggs identified Robinson's Discord username. The affidavit is by comparison the thinner record — and its affiant was not present.

(b) The mechanism claim is not overstated, it is contradicted. Same witness, on how the photo of the note was obtained: "following Lance's first interview at St. George PD, his phone is recovered at that point, taken into evidence. It is downloaded... The FBI transported that phone to the RCFL... The phone was downloaded through Cellebrite... SBI received a flash drive containing that download. From there, it went to a SIAC analyst... and at that point that this image of this letter was located." So the record contains BOTH the photograph-the-screen mechanic AND a phone handover AND a full forensic extraction. The profile's §3.7 trio — "the text thread, the photo of the note, and Discord material" — maps exactly onto Exhibits 16.1, 16.3 and 16.2. It is not broader than the record; it is the record. The Information of the same date corroborates in the State's own words: the roommate "provided those messages to police." The affidavit's screen-photo session is the earlier snapshot that preceded the extraction, not an alternative that displaces it.

(c) The timing claim is over-read. The affidavit never dates the roommate interview at all — it establishes only a ceiling. The "09/12/2025 04:00" on the signature line is the same date-time printed at line 10 as the arrest date/time, and the document separately carries "09/12/2025 07:18" in the submission block; deriving "the first interview was complete before 4 a.m." from a jumbled two-column OCR block is not supportable, and it conflicts with sworn testimony placing the interview in "the morning of September 12th." The affidavit also does not describe an "evening surrender" — it says Robinson was "encountered in-person by investigators in Washington County on September 12, early morning hours." Far from lacking a T1 anchor, the profile's Sept. 12 date is confirmed at T1 by the Day 4 transcript.

(d) "Entirely absent" is false on the profile's face: §3.4 already records that "Twiggs showed investigators Discord messages attributed to Robinson discussing the rifle and engraved bullets."

Also relevant to tier: the affidavit passage is not confined to the affidavit — Gov. Cox read it aloud almost verbatim at the September 12, 2025 press conference (banked as 2026-08-16_rev_fbi-captures-kirk-shooter.txt), so the content is additionally T2 named-official.

Search basis: all artifact searches ran through artifact_search.find(), which tests both the joined and hyphenated readings of the U+FFFE splits; the stem "jok" (capable of matching "made a joke on discord") returned hits under both readings in exactly five artifacts — the affidavit, the Information, day4.txt, and two Rev official-statement files — and zero in the profile apart from line 95. The patterns "photos of the screen" and "allowed investigators" hit only the affidavit and the Cox press conference; "photograph the phone" hits only day4.txt.

Direction-of-error note: adopting this finding would have pushed the file to claim MORE about a private individual on a weaker basis — that he "controlled what was displayed" and did not hand over his phone — when the sworn record shows he showed the phone, allowed it to be photographed, and the phone itself went into evidence and was fully extracted.


*Corrected form:*

Only one narrow remnant survives, and it should be entered at the recording layer only: the profile nowhere records that, per the probable-cause affidavit (and Gov. Cox reading it verbatim at the September 12, 2025 press conference, T2), the roommate told investigators that Robinson "made a joke on discord" before opening Discord and showing them the messages. Any addition must be worded as the affiant's third-hand paraphrase of a roommate the affidavit never names — the affiant testified he was not present at the interview — and never as Mr. Twiggs' quoted words or as his characterisation of "the Discord material" as a whole; on its face the clause characterises what Robinson posted.

Separately, a genuine completeness gap does exist but its source is the Day 4 transcript, not the affidavit: §3.4 and §3.7 do not record that the text thread and Discord exhibits (State's 16.1 and 16.2) originated as FBI photographs of Mr. Twiggs' phone screen taken by Agent Larson at the September 12 first interview at St. George PD, before his phone was taken into evidence and Cellebrite-extracted — and that the photo of the note (16.3) came from that extraction, not from the screen photos. Adding that upgrades §3.7 from AP (T3) to sworn testimony (T1) and makes the two-stage provenance explicit. The only phrase in §3.7 worth tightening is "handed over," which is the profile's characterisation of testimony that says his phone "is recovered at that point, taken into evidence."


**Verifier 2 — refuted=False**

A narrow residue survives, but the finding as written is mostly wrong, and one of its three legs is affirmatively contradicted by primary sources this library holds.

WHAT SURVIVES (verified by capable patterns, both readings where the folder requires it). Over the profile: `jok` returns exactly one hit — Twiggs' own text at line 95 — and `joke` as a noun returns zero, so a pattern able to match "made a joke on discord" was run and found nothing. `screen` returns one hit (line 199, "appear on screen", the redaction timecodes); `took photo`, `photograph(ed|s) (his|the) (phone|screen)`, `each message`, `message-by-message`, `one by one` all return zero. `04:00|4:00|4 a.m|4am|0400` and `early morning|overnight|pre-dawn|small hours` all return zero. So three specific items are genuinely not in the profile: the "joke" remark, the screen-photograph capture method, and any pre-dawn anchor.

WHAT IS REFUTED.
1. "Entirely absent" / "the profile has none of it" is false. §3.4 (line 111) states "Twiggs showed investigators Discord messages attributed to Robinson discussing the rifle and engraved bullets" — the very act the affidavit paragraph describes. §3.7 records the Sept. 12 interview, the buccal swab, the immunity and the material provided; §3.6 records that authorities had to locate him. The gap is a SOURCING gap (Tier 3 attribution, missing affidavit detail), not an absent account.
2. The mechanism leg is wrong on the record. The finding asserts "a mediated, message-by-message screen-photo session … not a handover of phone contents and not a forensic extraction," and calls §3.7 "broader than the record." day4.txt, Agent Davis under oath: "following Lance's first interview at St. George PD, his phone is recovered at that point, taken into evidence… The FBI transported that phone to the RCFL… The phone was downloaded through Cellebrite… SBI received a flash drive containing that download," and the photo of the note was located in that download. Same transcript: "he shows them the phone and then allows them to photograph the phone" (photos by FBI Agent Larson). The photographs and the extraction both happened; they are not alternatives. §3.7's "he handed over his phone contents: the text thread, the photo of the note, and Discord material" is therefore SUPPORTED at Tier 1, not overstated. The finding mistook one document's silence for the record's silence — the scope rule in the project's own objectivity mechanics.
3. "The only Tier 1 record of what Mr. Twiggs actually said and did at his first contact with investigators" is false. day3.txt and day4.txt are Tier 1 records of the same interview and are richer than the affidavit: location (St. George PD), interviewers (FBI Agents Larson and Ling), that he identified Robinson's Discord username (Tyler/Ikum), that he confirmed his own membership of the thread, that he identified the phone as his.
4. The timing attribution is wrong. The affidavit never dates the roommate interview at all. Its only timestamp is the trailing "09/12/2025 04:00" — the identical string the same form prints on its face as the arrest date/time ("On 09/12/2025 04:00 the defendant was arrested", line 10) — a form field, not narrative content. It yields a ceiling, not an anchor. The actual Tier 1 anchor is Davis's sworn testimony on day3: "The first time was the early morning hours of September 12th, and that occurred at St. George Police Department." So the profile's reliance on AP is a real weakness, but the finding names the wrong source for the fix and overstates what the affidavit carries.

LIVING-PERSONS OVERREACH. "His own characterisation of the Discord material as 'a joke'" enlarges the affiant's third-person paraphrase of one remark — "stated that his roommate (referring to Robinson) made a joke on discord" — into a stance by Mr. Twiggs about a body of evidence, and reads as minimisation by a man accused of nothing. It is not a quotation of him. Likewise, reading "as each message was shown by Robinson's roommate" as "the roommate controlled what was displayed" imports a gatekeeping implication the record contradicts: his phone was taken into evidence at that same interview and fully extracted, and the joke/meme framing the profile does carry sits where the record puts it — in Robinson's own text at line 104, "The f***in messages are mostly a big meme."


*Corrected form:*

Narrow, and stated as a sourcing gap rather than a missing account: (a) §3.4 records that Twiggs "showed investigators Discord messages" but sources it to CNN/AP (Tier 3) and never to the affidavit, and nowhere records the affidavit's added detail that the roommate told investigators Robinson "made a joke on discord" — a claim about the recording layer only, and about Robinson's conduct as Twiggs reported it, not a characterisation by Twiggs of the Discord material as a whole; (b) the profile nowhere records that at the first interview investigators photographed the phone screen as each message was shown — a detail independently confirmed at Tier 1 by Agent Davis on day4 ("he shows them the phone and then allows them to photograph the phone"; photos taken by FBI Agent Larson); (c) §3.7 dates the first interview only to "Sept. 12, 2025" on AP's authority when Tier 1 sworn testimony fixes it more precisely — Davis, day3: "The first time was the early morning hours of September 12th, and that occurred at St. George Police Department," with FBI Agents Larson and Ling present.

Expressly NOT part of the finding: §3.7's "he handed over his phone contents" is supported, not overstated — Davis testified the phone was "recovered at that point, taken into evidence," transported to the RCFL, "downloaded through Cellebrite," and the photo of the note was recovered from that download. The screen photographs and the forensic extraction both occurred; the affidavit describes only the first because it was signed hours later, before the download. And the affidavit does not date the interview: its "09/12/2025 04:00" is the form's arrest date/time field, so it supplies a ceiling consistent with Davis's "early morning hours," not an independent Tier 1 anchor.


### P2. [disqualification-and-officials] Cox's Sept-12 account of how the messages actually reached investigators — and the roommate's own "joke" characterisation — contradict §3.7's "handed over his phone contents"

- **Source file:** `~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/2026-08-16_rev_fbi-captures-kirk-shooter.txt`

**Source quote:**

> Investigators interviewed that roommate who stated that his roommate … made a joke on Discord. Investigators asked if he would show them the messages on Discord. He opened it and showed several messages to investigators and allowed investigators to take photos of the screen as each message was shown.

**Why it matters:**

This is the earliest official narration of the single most consequential act Mr. Twiggs performed, given by the Governor on 2025-09-12 — the day the profile dates his first interview. Three divergences. (1) MECHANISM: investigators identified him, investigators asked, he showed, and investigators photographed the screen message-by-message. That is not "handed over his phone contents." (2) HIS OWN FRAMING: he told investigators Robinson "made a joke on Discord" — the profile nowhere records that his initial characterisation to police was a joke. ABSENT — pattern "jok" returns exactly one hit in the profile, Twiggs' quoted text "You're joking, right????" (line 95), so the pattern is capable and the characterisation is genuinely missing. (3) THE NOTE PHOTO: neither Cox here nor the Information supports the claim that Twiggs handed over the photo of the note; the Information says only "Police found a photograph of this note." ABSENT — "phot" returns 6 substantive hits in the profile (lines 87, 89, 129, 266, 360), all of them the photo of the note or Twiggs photographing it, none describing investigators photographing his screen. Steelman recorded: the Information does say the roommate "provided those messages to police," which supports voluntary provision — but "provided" is narrower than "handed over his phone contents," and the profile's version outruns both sources. Bonus: Cox's investigator-initiated phrasing partially corroborates at Tier 2 the §3.6 relative claim the profile marks "not officially confirmed." Speaker basis: content — the speaker narrates the surrender chain and then says "I'll now turn the microphone over to the director of the FBI, Kash Patel."


**Verifier 1 — refuted=False**

The quote survives the does-the-source-say-it lens cleanly, but the finding's headline — that it CONTRADICTS §3.7's "handed over his phone contents" — is refuted by primary sources the finder never opened.

VERIFIED AS TO THE SOURCE. Line 11 of ~/Documents/Kirk Robinson Research/files - Court record/CR-ACQ-2026-07-27_court-record-artifacts/2026-08-16_rev_fbi-captures-kirk-shooter.txt carries the quoted words verbatim. The finder's ellipsis drops only "referring to Robinson," and the trailing "by Robinson's roommate"; neither alters sense. Speaker inferred from content, not label: the block is prepared narration opening "We got him," refers to "my state," is introduced by Speaker 1 as the Governor delivering remarks, closes "I'll now turn the microphone over to the director of the FBI, Kash Patel," and Patel's next line is "Thank you, Governor." Label and content agree. It is not a question and not counsel's characterisation. Date confirmed from content: "Good morning," suspect "taken into custody at 10 PM local time" last night, "33 hours" — morning of 2025-09-12.

REFUTED AS TO THE MECHANISM LIMB. Day 3 (Agent Brian Davis, direct by prosecutor Lauren Hunt; speakers inferred from question-vs-answer structure) is sworn T1: "Do you know if his phone was taken from him?" / "It was." / "When did that happen?" / "So It was during that interview with FBI at St. George Police Department. They conducted an interview of him and then he provided his phone. He did it voluntarily also, provided it to those agents." / "And that would've been on September 12th, 2025?" / "Correct." Day 4 completes the chain: a Cellebrite extraction WAS performed on Mr. Twiggs' phone; the FBI transported it to the RCFL; "The phone was downloaded through Cellebrite"; SBI received a flash drive of that download; a SIAC analyst searched it. So "handed over his phone contents" is substantially T1-SUPPORTED, on the very date §3.7 assigns, and traces to the same Davis testimony the surrounding sentence already cites. Cox's press-conference passage describes an EARLIER, DIFFERENT act — the on-screen showing of messages that investigators photographed (those photographs are State's Exhibits 16.1/16.2, "a photograph of Lance's phone," per Day 4). The two are sequential and complementary, not contradictory.

REFUTED AS TO THE NOTE-PHOTO LIMB AS FRAMED. Day 4: Hunt asks whether Exhibit 16.3 is "a fair and accurate representation of the photo law enforcement obtained from Mr. Twiggs's phone." The photo of the note did come from his phone, which he voluntarily provided. What is wrong is only the itemised verb — he did not affirmatively hand over that photo; an analyst located it inside the Cellebrite download.

SEARCH SCOPE STATED. All searches over the CR-ACQ artifacts folder used artifact_search.find(), which tests BOTH the join and hyphen readings of the U+FFFE defect. On the Information, "Discord" returns NULL under BOTH readings — so the finder is right that the Information never mentions Discord — and "provid" returns only "the roommate told police that the roommate received messages from Robinson about the shooting and provided those messages to police"; "photograph" returns only "Police found a photograph of this note"; "hand" and "turn over" return null under both readings. But that null is a fact about two documents, not about the record: the finder concluded "the profile's version outruns both sources" without checking the five hearing transcripts this library holds, which is where the support actually lives. Per project rule, absence is only evidence of absence where the search space would necessarily contain the record; it would not here.

ABSENCE CLAIMS RE-RUN AGAINST THE PROFILE (these hold). "jok" returns exactly one hit in lance-twiggs-comprehensive-profile.md — line 95, Twiggs' quoted text "You're joking, right????" — so the pattern is demonstrably capable of matching "joke" and the roommate's initial characterisation is genuinely unrecorded. "phot" returns hits at lines 87, 89, 129, 212, 266, 333, 360 (the finder listed five lines while saying six, and omitted line 212's "Shown surveillance photos"); none describes investigators photographing his screen message-by-message, so that step is genuinely missing too.

BONUS CLAIM REFUTED. Cox's "Investigators identified an individual as the roommate… Investigators interviewed that roommate" adds nothing beyond the Information's existing T1 "Police interviewed Robinson's roommate." Neither corroborates the distinctive content of the §3.6 relative claim — that authorities "had to locate him and bring him in for questioning," implying he did not come forward. Davis's sworn account has him providing his phone voluntarily, and Cox's own next sentence has him showing messages and allowing photographs. The §3.6 "not officially confirmed" marking should stand unchanged.

WHAT SURVIVES. Two verified recording-layer completeness gaps and one wording overstatement, all narrow, none amounting to a contradiction. Set refuted=false only on that basis.

RESIDUAL SCOPE. I did not re-verify the profile's AP/NewsNation/ABC4 secondary citations, the April 20 2026 second-interview content, or whether any warrant or consent form governed the phone's retention after the voluntary handover; Davis's "provided… voluntarily" is the only sworn account of the taking that I read.


*Corrected form:*

Drop the contradiction claim entirely. §3.7's "handed over his phone contents" is T1-supported and should stand, with a citation added: SBI Agent Brian Davis testified that during the September 12, 2025 first interview at St. George Police Department, Mr. Twiggs "provided his phone. He did it voluntarily also, provided it to those agents" (Day 3 transcript, T1), and that a Cellebrite extraction of that phone was performed at the RCFL, with the download delivered to SBI on a flash drive (Day 4 transcript, T1).

What remains is narrower, and is a completeness note on §3.7 rather than a correction of it:

(1) ADD, as a T2 recording-layer item attributed to Cox and not adopted: at the September 12, 2025 press conference the Governor narrated that investigators identified Robinson's roommate, interviewed him, and that the roommate stated Robinson "made a joke on Discord"; investigators then asked to see the Discord messages, "He opened it and showed several messages to investigators and allowed investigators to take photos of the screen as each message was shown." Record this as Cox's second-hand paraphrase of what the roommate told investigators — it is not a quotation of Mr. Twiggs' own words, and it should carry no evidentiary weight as to his state of mind. Pattern "jok" is capable (it matches line 95's "joking") and returns only that one hit, so the characterisation is confirmed absent.

(2) ADD the screen-photography step to the mechanism, which the profile omits: before the phone was provided, investigators photographed the messages on his screen one at a time, and those photographs became the exhibits — Day 4 Davis identifies State's Exhibit 16.1 as "a photograph of screenshots of text messages between Tyler Robinson and Lance Twiggs… a photograph of Lance's phone." Confirmed absent: no "phot" hit in the profile describes investigators photographing his screen.

(3) NARROW the itemised list. Replace "He handed over his phone contents: the text thread, the photo of the note, and Discord material" with a two-step description that does not assign him an affirmative act he did not perform: he showed messages on his device, which investigators photographed, and he then voluntarily provided the phone itself, from whose forensic download a SIAC analyst subsequently located the image of the note. Per Day 4, the note photo was found by analyst search of the extraction — not selected and handed over by him. The Information supports only the narrower "provided those messages to police," and never mentions Discord at all (null under BOTH the join and hyphen readings).

(4) Leave §3.6 unchanged. Cox's "investigators identified… investigators interviewed" phrasing does not corroborate the relative's claim that authorities had to locate him and bring him in; the Information already says "Police interviewed Robinson's roommate" at T1, and Davis's testimony describes voluntary provision. The "not officially confirmed" marking stands.


**Verifier 2 — refuted=True**

REFUTED as framed. The finding's headline — that the Sept-12 account "contradicts" §3.7's "handed over his phone contents" — collapses against T1 sworn testimony the finder never reached. Searches over the artifact folder used artifact_search.find(), which matches BOTH the joined and hyphenated U+FFFE readings; every hit below returned under both readings, so the nulls and hits are real under both.

(1) MECHANISM — refuted at T1. Day 3 (2026-07-08), SBI Agent Brian Davis, examined by prosecutor Lauren Hunt (speaker inferred from content: Hunt asks, Davis answers as case agent about investigative steps): Q "Do you know if his phone was taken from him?" A "It was." Q "When did that happen?" A "So It was during that interview with FBI at St. George Police Department. They conducted an interview of him and then **he provided his phone. He did it voluntarily also, provided it to those agents**." (day3.txt, 01:02:53–01:03:14; agents named at 01:02:21 as FBI Larson and Ling.) So a voluntary handover of the phone happened at the very interview the finder cites. Cox's screen-photography passage and the handover are SEQUENTIAL STEPS OF ONE INTERVIEW, not competing accounts. Day 4 confirms the first step and dates it: "during that first interview on September 12th, physically, he shows them the phone and then allows them to photograph the phone" (day4.txt, 02:37:42–02:37:48; photos taken by FBI Agent Larson, 02:37:34).

(2) THE NOTE PHOTO — refuted as framed. Day 4, Davis on how law enforcement got the note image: "following Lance's first interview at St. George PD, his phone is recovered at that point, taken into evidence… The FBI transported that phone to the RCFL… downloaded through Cellebrite… it went to a SIAC analyst to go through that data of his phone… And at that point that this image of this letter was located" (day4.txt, 02:58:03–02:59:05); Exhibit 16.3 is "the photo law enforcement obtained from Mr. Twiggs's phone" (03:00:38), and Sgt. Faumuina identifies "the image of a note that was recovered from Lance Twiggs' phone" (day4.txt, 05:11:11). The note photo therefore came off the phone he voluntarily provided. §3.7 does not "outrun both sources"; it compresses a documented chain.

(3) SOURCE UNDER-ATTRIBUTED. The identical narration is not merely Cox at T2 — it is verbatim in the AFFIDAVIT OF PROBABLE CAUSE, a primary filing, affiant "UTBDAVIS - Brian Davis," entered 09/12/2025 07:18: "Investigators interviewed the roommate, who stated that his roommate (referring to Robinson) made a joke on discord. Investigators asked if he would show them the messages on discord, he opened it and showed several messages…" (2025-09-16_affidavit-of-probable-cause_dc27880530.txt, lines 114–118). The finding graded its own best evidence too low.

(4) PROFILE DOES RECORD THE SHOW-MECHANISM. §3.4 (line 111): "Twiggs **showed investigators** Discord messages attributed to Robinson discussing the rifle and engraved bullets." The show-verb is in the file.

WHAT GENUINELY SURVIVES is limb 2 only, and it is not a contradiction of §3.7 — it is an unrelated completeness gap. Pattern "jok" returns EXACTLY ONE hit in the profile (line 95, Twiggs' own text "You're joking, right????"), so the pattern is capable and the "made a joke on discord" statement is truly absent; synonym sweep confirms it (meme = 1 hit, Robinson's own words at line 104; prank/kidding/hoax/jest/sarcas = 0). Also genuinely absent: Larson, Cellebrite, RCFL, SIAC, extract, seiz, "into evidence" (all 0 hits).

OVERSTATEMENT ABOUT A MAN ACCUSED OF NOTHING. The finder glosses the affidavit as "HIS OWN FRAMING… his initial characterisation to police was a joke," which imputes a minimising stance. The source says only that he stated ROBINSON "made a joke on discord" — it does not identify which message, does not say he applied that word to the confession, the note or the texts, and establishes nothing about his state of mind. Note also that the finder's preferred correction would make §3.7 claim LESS cooperation than the T1 record supports; the direction that claims less about Mr. Twiggs here is the profile's existing "handed over," which Davis's "he provided his phone… voluntarily" independently carries.


*Corrected form:*

Two narrow, verified items survive; the contradiction claim does not.

(a) COMPLETENESS, NOT CONTRADICTION — §3.7 compresses a three-step acquisition into one clause. Supported narrower text: "At the September 12, 2025 interview at St. George Police Department (FBI Agents Larson and Ling), Twiggs showed investigators the messages on his phone and allowed Agent Larson to photograph the screen as each message was displayed, and then voluntarily provided the phone itself to the agents. The phone was taken into evidence, transported by the FBI to the Regional Computer Forensic Laboratory in Salt Lake City and downloaded via Cellebrite; a DPS SIAC analyst subsequently located the image of Robinson's note within that download. Twiggs therefore did not hand over the photo of the note as a discrete item — it was recovered by forensic examination of the phone he had voluntarily provided." Tier 1 (affidavit of probable cause, Brian Davis, entered 2025-09-12; Davis testimony day3 01:02:53–01:03:14 and day4 02:37:42–02:37:48, 02:58:03–02:59:05, 03:00:38; Faumuina day4 05:11:11). Confidence: High. Speakers inferred from content per standing hazard — Hunt/McBride ask, Davis and Faumuina answer as witnesses; Rev labels not relied on.

(b) A GENUINE OMISSION, stated at the width the source carries — the profile nowhere records the statement that prompted investigators to ask for the Discord messages. Supported narrower text: "Both the affidavit of probable cause (T1) and Gov. Cox's September 12, 2025 remarks (T2) record that the roommate told investigators Robinson 'made a joke on discord,' after which investigators asked if he would show them those messages. The record does not identify which message he was describing and does not establish that he characterised Robinson's confession, the note or the texts as a joke." Confidence: High for the fact of the statement; the referent is unestablished. Do NOT render this as "his initial characterisation to police was a joke."

(c) The finding's bonus should also be narrowed: the affidavit's "Investigators identified an individual as the roommate of Robinson. Investigators interviewed the roommate" corroborates at T1 that the contact was investigator-initiated. It does not corroborate the §3.6 relative's stronger "authorities initially had to locate him and bring him in," which implies difficulty or reluctance the record does not carry — and Davis's "he provided his phone… voluntarily" cuts the other way.

### Block **BLOCK S3 — per-claim tier re-marking of §3 (September 10–12, 2025: The Critical 72 Hours)**
Prepared 2026-08-16. Search method: every transcript/artifact search ran through `artifact_search.find()`, which tests **both** the joined and hyphenated readings of the U+FFFE line-break defect; every null reported below held under **both** readings, and I name the matching-capable pattern in each case.

**Headline: §3 is the most upgradeable block in the file.** The methodological note ("most court-record content below is accessed through news reporting") is now false for the great majority of §3. Roughly three-quarters of §3's substantive claims move from *T1 via T3* to **direct T1**, on four held artifacts: the Information (2025-09-16), the affidavit of probable cause (2025-09-16), the Day-3/Day-4 preliminary-hearing transcripts, and the defence's 2026-08-11 briefing.

**The single largest upgrade is that §3.1, §3.2 (fate of note), and §3.5 rest on a sworn Rule 1102 statement the library now holds in transcript.** The April 20, 2026 interview played as State's Exhibit 16 opens with the interviewer placing Twiggs under oath, giving the false-statement (Class A misdemeanor) admonition, and confirming state and federal immunity agreements on the record. It is not "an interview reported by NewsNation"; it is sworn testimony taken in lieu of live testimony under Utah R. Evid. 1102. **Speaker-identification basis (Rev labels are not evidence):** the labelled speaker states his own name ("My name is Lance Twiggs"), is sworn, and answers rather than asks throughout; Agent Brian Davis then testified under oath that he was physically present at that interview and that Exhibit 16 fairly and accurately depicts it (day4 02:34:16–02:34:32). The questioner is never named in the recording as transcribed; Davis testified the admonition was given by Ryan McBride (day3 01:06:08) and defence counsel Novak characterised the video as "Mr. McBride testifying and Mr. Twiggs agreeing" (day3) — that inference, not the Rev label, is the basis for reading "Speaker 2"/"Speaker 4" as McBride.

**Four corrections the primary sources force:**

1. **§3.5 — "acting erratically" is not Twiggs' characterisation.** "So he acted erratically. Was he pacing?" is the *interviewer's* question; Twiggs' answer opens "**No**, he was walking around a lot" (day4 02:25:32–02:26:10). The profile places a questioner's premise, which the witness partly rejected, inside a paragraph captioned "From Twiggs' recorded interview." This is the same layer failure as the corrected Q-135 item in §3.3 — a word attributed to a private individual that he did not say.

2. **§3.2 — what was displayed in court as "the note" was Twiggs' phone photograph, not the physical note.** State's Exhibit 16.3 is the image recovered from the Cellebrite extraction of Twiggs' seized phone by a SIAC analyst (Davis, day4 02:58:07–03:00:27), published **in the courtroom only, not videoed**. The physical burnt paper is a separate exhibit (24), and the transcript's answer to whether the two are consistent is **[inaudible 05:15:51]** — so the held record does *not* carry the identification of the burnt paper as that note, and carries nothing at all for "pieced together by forensics."

3. **§3.2 — "who burned the note, when, and where has not been publicly established" is now half-answered.** Sgt. Faumuina testified the burned paper was recovered **from the trash can in the kitchen** of the residence Robinson and Twiggs shared, and sent to the FBI (day4 05:01:00, 05:09:28). Davis testified that search warrant was executed starting **about 8:00 a.m. on September 12** (day3 48:14). Where is established; when is bracketed (after ~11 p.m. Sept 10, before ~8 a.m. Sept 12); who is not.

4. **§3.7 — "received limited-use immunity … before cooperating" is not carried.** The held record ties use immunity (Utah County Attorney's Office and U.S. Attorney's Office) to the **April 20, 2026** statement (Davis, day3 01:04:13–01:05:19; the recording's own preamble). Nothing in the held record places any immunity before the September 12, 2025 interview, at which Twiggs had already shown the messages and surrendered his phone. The clause imports a sequence the record does not supply.

**New T1 content §3 does not currently contain.** The Day-4 in-court reading of Exhibit 16.1 includes a message absent from the Information: immediately after Twiggs' "What? … You're joking, right?", Robinson replies **"Fuck, I tried to delete that."** (day4 02:42:20). This bears directly on §3.2's auto-text reasoning and belongs in the §3.3 recording layer. The reading also supplies "Will update you by midnight", "Does anyone else know?" / "Not to my knowledge", "There was a four digit serial on it" (in tension with the Information's ellipsed "idek if it had a serial number"), and Twiggs' own turns ("All right, stay safe", "Are you okay still?", "Drive safe"). Davis further dated the thread at both ends from the Cellebrite extraction: first text **Sept 10, 11:00 p.m.**, last **≈7:42 p.m. Sept 11** (day4 02:40:06, 02:49:52).

**What still cannot be upgraded, with search scope stated.** The full text of the note beyond two fragments; "set/sent an auto text"; the "Luna" salutation; "likely dead, or facing a lengthy prison sentence"; "Please try and find joy in this life." Patterns `lengthy prison`, `find joy`, `I am so sorry`, `on a mission`, `lived our lives`, `likely dead`, `auto.?text|automated message|auto text`, and the stem `auto` were run across all twelve held artifacts (five transcripts, Information, affidavit, defence brief, disqualification opposition, contempt transcript, docket, and the four Rev press-conference transcripts) and returned nothing under either reading; each of those patterns is capable of matching the phrase it is testing. The library holds **no unsealed April 2026 search warrant**, which is the only route to the note's verbatim text — that remains a Tier 1 acquisition target and everything resting on it stays **T1 via T3**. Likewise `Zinn` (capable of matching the surname) is a real null across all held artifacts: the "crazy old dude" identification remains inference, correctly flagged in the file. `Bongino` is a real null across all four held press-conference transcripts; Gov. Cox, by contrast, is now T2-direct — he read the affidavit's roommate/Discord passage aloud on 2025-09-12.

**One scoped null I will not overstate.** Whether the Discord thread (Exhibit 16.2) was read aloud on Day 4: no reading of its content appears anywhere in the day4 transcript, but there is an **inaudible gap of about 2m39s (02:54:29 → 02:57:08)** immediately after its admission. The AP-vs-KUTV display question in §3.4 nonetheless resolves at T1 in a way the profile's current text gets backwards — the exhibits *were* displayed, and the difference is per-exhibit: the text thread (16.1) was published to the gallery **and** captured by the media camera and read message-by-message; the Discord thread (16.2) was published **to the gallery only, barred from electronic distribution**, and not read in the transcript; the note photo (16.3) was published to the courtroom only and not videoed.

**Residual scope of this block.** I re-verified §3 only. I did not re-verify §1, §2, §4–§13, and I did not open the PDFs behind the .txt artifacts. I did not attempt to obtain the April 2026 search warrants, the Sept 12, 2025 interview recording, or the exhibits themselves (16.1–16.4, 24) — every claim above about an exhibit's *content* is carried by the transcript's record of what was read or described, not by reading the exhibit. Two different witnesses named Faumuina appear in this record (Sgt. Faumuina, SBI crime scene, day4; Jennifer Faumuina, DNA analyst, day2) and are kept distinct above.

SEARCH-HAZARD COMPLIANCE. Every search over this folder was run through artifact_search.find(), which matches BOTH the joined and hyphenated readings of the U+FFFE split. audit() reports the five transcript days and the condensed files carry 0 NULs and 0 U+FFFE splits, so the two readings are byte-identical there; I ran both anyway. Nulls asserted below and the pattern that could have matched them: "residu" (capable of matching "residue"/"gunshot residue") = 0 hits, all five days, both readings; "Twiggs" = 0 hits in the Information and in the affidavit of probable cause, both readings (he is "the roommate" throughout); "Twiggs" = 0 hits in the docket, both readings, and the docket ends 2026-03-15 so it cannot reach any June/July 2026 filing; "Texas" in a Twiggs sense = hits only in Graf's ruling (the day3/day4 hits are Estes v. Texas); "not be excluded" / "did not identify" = 0 in day2.

REV-LABEL DISCIPLINE. Every speaker below was inferred from content, not from the label: Bakker is identified by her own answers (STRmix, likelihood ratios, "DNA Casework Unit of the FBI laboratory… Quantico"); Burt and McBride by question-versus-answer and by who moved what; Graf by the fact that the turns are rulings and orders. Where I name a person I say what in the content fixes it.

THREE HEADLINE OUTCOMES OF THIS PASS:
1. §5's posture description is wrong at T1. Amanda Bakker was a DEFENCE witness, called out of order — the State's own counsel said so on the record ("we're going to call an FBI DNA analyst out of order. It's a defense witness"). Burt's questioning was DIRECT, not cross. The profile's "Bakker on cross-examination … made admissions properly labeled as such" must be struck.
2. §4's "authorities have consistently said they do not believe he participated" does not survive contact with the held T2 record. Cox supports it; Jeff Gray, at the charging press conference, three times refused to say the roommate had no part or that no charges could follow.
3. §6's closing premise is date-stale. Day 5 ends with the preliminary hearing CONTINUED to 2026-09-01 for up to four hours of argument, and Graf's quash order is expressly renewable without prejudice. The hearing did not conclude on 2026-07-10.

REFINEMENT 2, and it matters for §4 and §6.3: "this denial is without prejudice and subject to renewal… If the State's presentation materially differs from the proffer presented before the court, or if defendant can identify specific testimony reasonably likely to negate probable cause, defendant may renew the request." | §6.2 bullet 3 | "He relied on State v. The court references Lopez only because of its discussion of reasonableness informs that analysis. Whether Lopez formally governs subpoenas directed to non-victim witnesses may not be resolved to decide the present motion. The court's ruling does not depend upon extending Lopez beyond its facts… while the court's holding rests independently upon Rule 14A2, Rule 7B, and the limited functions of a preliminary hearing." The court also ACCEPTED the defence's distinguishing point: "The court agrees that Lopez cannot be read without attention to that context" (victim status). This is a Rule 14(a)(2) reasonableness holding, not a Lopez holding — a distinction that matters to any assessment of how orthodox or how extendable the ruling is. |

Timmerman; a relaxed application at the probable-cause stage of confrontation, counsel, due process and the Eighth Amendment; and the holding that the Rule 1102 presumption of reliability goes to admissibility only, leaving credibility and sufficiency with the magistrate. |

(a) Purpose: 2026-08-11_defendant-prelim-hearing-briefing states it in terms — 'The presence of a screwdriver on the roof does not establish that the rifle was taken apart and put back together any more than it establishes that the screwdriver was intended to take the rifle apart after the shooting. A party's post-hearing brief asserting the State adduced no such evidence is stronger than the profile's own inference of silence. (b) Collection: day2 Faumuina at 05:02:16–05:09:01 (Item 8, 'Screwdriver from perch', collected from the rooftop, sent to the FBI lab); day4 Faumuina at 04:35:59 (a screwdriver among the rooftop items). (c) Mechanism: unaddressed, and now explained by day2 at 07:21:25 — the examiner did not derive Twiggs' presence from a statistic at all, so no transfer mechanism was ever in issue. CAUTION for anyone citing day1 on this: at 01:43:39 the Rev label reads 'Richard Novak' on answers that are plainly a witness's ('It was a red and black screwdriver'; 'That's a picture of the screwdriver that I saw that day on top of the rooftop of the Losee building') under Grunander's direct examination. Content wins; the label is defence counsel's name on a State witness's testimony. |
| §11 Q4 | The end and current character of the Robinson–Twiggs relationship — undocumented beyond the 'former partner' designation. | T3 | T1 for the relationship's START; UNCHANGED (still undocumented) for its end | PARTIAL UPGRADE, and the open question survives. T1: day4.txt video at 02:14:36–02:14:48 — 'at some point your relationship progressed to something more than just roommates, is that right?' … 'I think we started dating two or three months after he moved in.' NULLS with capable patterns: 'romantic' returns zero in day4; 'former partner' returns zero across day1–day5, both readings. The June 2026 contempt ruling uses the past tense of record ('allegedly resided with and was in a romantic relationship with Defendant AT THE TIME of the alleged [offences]') — which dates the relationship to the offence period without stating its end. Living-persons note: this is the one open question in §11 whose answer is nobody's business unless it bears on credibility at trial; keep it framed that way. |
| §11 Q5 | Whether Twiggs will testify live at trial, where confrontation rights attach fully; his credibility, immunity terms, and the September 12 interview contents would be tested for the first time. | T3 | T1 for the context; UNCHANGED as an open question (necessarily — it is about a future event) | CONTEXT UPGRADED. 2026-06-23 contempt transcript: the State 'does not intend to call Mr. Twiggs to testify live at the preliminary hearing' and planned to proceed on the Rule 1102 recording; the defence sought live cross 'concerning the reliability and credibility of his statements, his immunity agreements with the State and federal prosecutors, his state of mind'; the application was denied and the subpoena quashed, the court reasoning that bias/immunity/inconsistency evidence 'may be highly relevant at trial.' So the question is not open in the sense of unaddressed — it is deferred by an order the library now holds. Frame it as deferred, and remember the antecedent: there is no trial unless Robinson is bound over, and he has not been. |
| §11 Q6 | The contents of the redacted portions of the April 20 interview and 'the full Sept. 12 interview, which Judge Graf reviewed privately for the probable-cause decision.' | T1 via T3, with a v1.2 posture correction | T1 for the redaction set and the posture; the SEPTEMBER 12 half of the sentence is NOT supported | UPGRADE + CORRECTION. (a) Redaction set: T1, see §7.2. (b) Posture: fully T1 — day4.txt sets the schedule from the bench (State's brief 'by the 28th of July'; Ms. Visser 'We need two weeks after that. So August 11th'; 'It would be seven days after that … the 18th?' → 'August 18th'; 'for oral argument … September 1st in the morning'), and day5.txt closes: 'this preliminary hearing is continued to September 1st at 10:00 AM for an in-person hearing. The court will allocate up to four hours total for argument … this proceeding is adjourned until September 1st at 10:00 AM.' The v1.2 posture correction is confirmed at Tier 1. (c) THE UNSUPPORTED CLAUSE: what Graf received unredacted and undertook to re-review is State's Exhibit 16 — the APRIL 20 recording (day4 at ~55:38; day3 at 02:43:44). Nothing in the held record shows the September 12, 2025 recording was offered, admitted, or reviewed by the court; the State's route for that interview was Agent Davis's testimony about it plus Exhibit 16.1, the photographs taken during it (day4 at 02:37:18–02:37:48). State's Exhibit 14, admitted but expressly not published (day3 at 56:40), is a different witness's Rule 1102 statement, not the September 12 Twiggs interview. Split the sentence: the redacted April 20 material is withheld from PUBLICATION but is before the court; the September 12 recording is, so far as the held record shows, before neither. |

**Dropped:** (1) POSTURE CONTRADICTION, the strongest item below the cap — the profile says "Bakker on cross-examination (defense attorney Michael Burt) made admissions properly labeled as such," but Burt CALLED her: "Your Honor, out of order, the defense would be calling Amanda Bakker," the court said "we are still with Mr. Burt and Ms. Baker on direct examination," and McBride said "I need to cross-examine this witness" — so the "admissions" were answers on the defence's own direct, and the §5.1 steelman quotes came under the STATE's cross plus defence redirect; this inverts the evidentiary character of the whole section. (2) PCAST: both items' minor contributor — Mr. Twiggs on both — fell below the 20% floor PCAST flagged as not foundationally validated (5% on the towel, 11% on the screwdriver); Bakker's rebuttal was "in this case it was also only a mixture of two individuals," which finding 1 destabilises for item 7; profile has no "PCAST" and no "20%". (3) Items 1–6, six swabs from "the northeast corner of the loci [Losee] building" (possible smudges, hand swipe, sweat drops), were entirely CONSUMED and yielded NO DNA — "There was nothing for me to compare to" — ~3 microlitres of extract returned, which she would not expect to yield DNA; bears directly on §5.2's on-roof-evidence point. (4) Item 55, the stairwell railing (two swabs combined into one before testing), produced "DNA from more than five individuals, so I could not provide a conclusion." (5) Item 9, the backpack, was excluded as a bystander's — lab notes recorded "The backpack belongs to Kirk's detailees" — and no comparisons were run. (6) Her verbal scale requires an LR of at least 2 for even "limited support for inclusion," so Twiggs' LR of 1 sits BELOW the lowest inclusion rung, not merely at "uninformative." (7) The elimination designation came from the FIELD, not the lab — "given that the field submitted him as an elimination" — investigators told her "that towel came from Mr. Robinson's home and I should utilize Mr. Twiggs as an elimination," while she knew the towel "was found outside of a home." (8) In the one Twiggs-alone run the propositions were set properly against him — HP = "sample 61… Twiggs's known sample", HD left blank (= unknown unrelated) — i.e. that run genuinely tested him and returned 1. (9) Item identifiers the profile lacks: item 7 = "Towel from around Mauser or rifle, Serial Number 8863"; item 8 = "Screwdriver from perch"; item 8.1 = swabs of the handle area; Twiggs' known buccal standard = "sample 61". (10) Item 7 gave a FULL 23-locus profile; item 8 did not. (11) Degradation: item 8 showed a "ski slope" pattern, item 7 "slight"; on the State's cross she said neither affected her conclusions. (12) The sequencing limitation applies to Mr. Twiggs too — Burt: "she can't match Mr. Robinson to the questioned samples, or for that matter, Mr. Twigg's sample"; Bakker: "I only utilize length differences… The FBI laboratory does not use sequencing for nuclear DNA testing, nor am I qualified in it" (isoalleles 17A/17B/17C etc., Baker 34/35). (13) Timeline: evidence first received 2025-09-11, three-contributor reading ~09-12, Twiggs' elimination sample received and conclusion changed 09-13, report issued 09-15 — his swab was in FBI hands and compared within a day of his 09-12 interview. (14) Named participants absent from the profile: FBI SA Folger/Fulger, "Carmack", Utah SBI SA Dave Hull (told the results on 09-13), and technical reviewer Tara Benson, whose independent read of item 7 was TWO contributors. (15) Faumuina said only that Twiggs' DNA "was obtained by law enforcement officers" — day2 does not establish voluntariness, which the profile asserts ("voluntarily provided buccal swab", sourced to OANN/Fox). (16) Rev renders McBride's screwdriver recap as Robinson "better aligned with that 79% contributor" in the same breath as his own question said 89% — a transcript-internal discrepancy at the exact figure the profile carries as ~89%. (17) The case was worked at "Priority 1 Level… 24 hours a day, seven days a week", with testing begun while evidence was still being inventoried and multiple reviewers rotating — "which is not usual for a case", in her words. (18) The State's own relevance position, repeated across five objections, was that reliability of the DNA is a Rule 702 question for a later hearing and not for probable cause; the court sustained in part each time and said "I want to keep it limited to one mile as opposed to a hundred miles" — so the DNA reliability record on day 2 is deliberately truncated, which no part of the profile states.

Robinson DID message a group chat Twiggs was in on 10 September (\"he sent that message in the group chat I mentioned, but he didn't message me directly at all that day\"), qualifying §3.1's \"did not hear from Robinson again until about 11 p.m.\"
Twiggs slept until \"Probably 12:00 to 1:00 PM\" on 10 September and did not work that day — absent context for the 11 p.m. first contact and for what he could have known during the day.
Twiggs did not hand over the Dremel — \"I just told him where it was\" — and warned Robinson \"to make sure he doesn't set off a bullet on accident in the house\"; §7.3's \"asked to borrow a Dremel tool\" overstates the transaction and drops his warning.
Twiggs hedged the timing twice (\"I don't remember\"; the trip had been discussed \"for a couple months\"; the Dremel request \"at most a month before\") — §7.3's flat \"about a month before the shooting\" drops the hedge and hardens his outside bound into a point estimate.
The burnt paper was recovered \"in the trash can located in the kitchen\" of the shared residence (Faumuina) — §3.2 and §11.1 say where the note was burned \"has not been publicly established.\"
An entire second body of DNA statistics the profile's §5 never mentions: ATF reports (Exhibits 30 and 33) on the rifle's stock/grips, bolt, butt plate, trigger, fore-end, barrel and scope, on cartridges from the gun, and on the household Dremel and its bits — every proposition reads \"Tyler Robinson … and N unrelated, unknown individuals,\" with Mr. Twiggs conditioned into none of them, including the Dremel he called part of \"our random tools.\"
Cellebrite/Discord timings absent from the profile: first text 10 Sept 11:00 PM; last text \"just before 8:00 PM on September 11th… I think 7:42 PM\"; Robinson's first Discord message 7:57 PM on 11 Sept; arrival at the sheriff's office ~9:00 PM.
The State recorded that Mr. Twiggs was represented during the April-20 statement and that \"his counsel made no objections to this part of the interview\" — §6.1 says only that he retained counsel who declined subpoena service.
Twiggs said he was without his phone \"after that for a while\" and \"still am not using social media\" — a first-person Tier 1 basis for §9's \"went almost entirely dark,\" which the profile currently supports with media speculation.
Agent Davis, who authenticated every Twiggs exhibit, drew no cross-examination at all (\"No cross-examination for Agent Davis\") — a fact about how the Twiggs material entered the probable-cause record that §6's confrontation analysis does not mention.
Twiggs identified Robinson's Discord handle (\"Tyler Icum\" / \"Tyler/Ikum\") and described the server as Robinson's weekly D&D friend group — absent from §3.4.
Minor variant: Davis read \"they grabbed some crazy old dude and interrogated someone in similar clothing\"; the profile has \"then interrogated\" — noted only because §3.2 already tracks transcription variants.
