Graf's Day-5 findings on that incident: Exhibit 16 "appeared on the live stream for approximately three and one-half seconds"; the displayed portion "did not contain the full note, and consisted of approximately 78 words"; "Approximately 15 of those words were included in the information filed on September 16th, 2026" (the year is a misstatement or Rev error — the Information is 2025-09-16).
3. TIER 1 ACQUISITION PATH for §11.2/§3.2's unresolved note wording: media counsel stated on the record that "the full text, every word of that exhibit, is contained in a search warrant in this case that was initially filed on September 17th... in the Fourth District, but in a different court... on March 17th... released in full. On page seven of that search warrant is the full text of this exhibit."
4. Date conflict inside Day 5 on that same warrant: media counsel says released "March 16th, March 17th"; Graf's finding says the full text "had previously been made public through a search warrant that was publicly available on or before April 2026" — the profile cites ABC4 "April 2026" for the unsealed warrants.
5. The defence renewed its motion to bar ALL electronic media coverage (docket 305) as a sanction; denied — "The analysis underlying the court's prior ruling remains unchanged."
6. Graf expressly RESERVED the camera question for September 1 and refused a pre-ruling: "It is not before the court. There has not been a request for electronic media coverage and the court won't issue a pre-ruling" — so 09-01 may or may not be broadcast.
7. Victim's counsel objected that the exhibit sanction left the Kirk family unable to follow the evidence ("presence without being able to see the exhibits... is not meaningful"); Graf held only the attorneys may view exhibits.
8. State's Exhibit 12.1 was played on Day 5 with the livestream cut to audio only, no audio track on the exhibit itself, and still photography of monitors barred — its content is never identified on the record; it was played at the victim family's request.
9. Mr. Robinson personally elected not to testify at the preliminary hearing; the court noted him "nodding in the affirmative."
10. The defence put on a case-in-chief at the preliminary hearing — "a little over five hours" of defence witnesses per the State, with experts who would have had to be flown "out from the East Coast"; the profile records no defence evidence at all.
11. Defence characterization of how the DNA evidence entered: "the state presented DNA reports through a non-expert. They selectively read into the record a portion of those reports, which left an extremely misleading impression of the significance of that evidence" — bears directly on how §5's DNA content reached the record.
12. The State's probable-cause authority chain, quoted at length: State v. Schmidt, Pledger, Hester, Virgin, Droesbeke and Lopez, with "the magistrate may not sift or weigh the evidence" — the legal reason no Day-5 DNA-limitations testimony can change the bind-over outcome.
13. A waiver dispute the profile lacks: the defence said the State had told it there would be "no standing objection" to these witnesses and that any such objection is now waived; the State nonetheless flagged a "likely forthcoming standing objection" and later objected four times.
14. ATF uses a FIVE-category conclusion scale and no verbal qualifiers at all — a different reporting regime from the FBI verbal scale §5.1 quotes; three relevance objections cut off the defence's next-generation-sequencing / isoallele critique (17A/17B/17C at locus D3), so that critique is not in the record.
15. Nine samples were entirely CONSUMED in ATF testing (1.4, 1.6, 1.9, 1.10, 1.12, 2.1, 3.1, 4.1, 5.1), extracts retained for nine others — with testimony on the 1996 NRC split-sample policy and "a wrongfully accused person's best insurance... is the opportunity to have the testing repeated."
16. Matthew Robinson (the defendant's father) had "support for inclusion" on the swabs of the BOLT of the rifle and on item 1.6 — a third party's DNA on the weapon, explained by exactly the household-association logic applied to Mr. Twiggs; Amber Robinson was the third elimination donor. Neither parent appears in the profile as a DNA elimination source.
17. Elimination samples were requested from the investigating AGENTS but never received — relevant to contamination, and absent from the profile.
18. ATF's Robinson figures, capped: "at least one trillion times more likely" on the trigger/trigger-guard swab (1.3) and on 1.13 + 2.1 (protected underside of the receiver; one fired 30-06 cartridge case), with the probability statistic "less than one in a trillion" — the lab caps at one trillion by protocol.
19. Item 1.3 (trigger/trigger guard) was DEGRADED, "consistent with at least four contributors," its major profile carrying the most degradation, and its minor component "not suitable for comparison"; item 1.1 (stock) had TWO major contributors among at least four.
20. ATF SOP language put to the witness: "approximately 70% of four-person mixtures present as two- or three-person mixtures based solely on number of alleles present"; and the agreed NIST caveat "The true number of contributors of DNA to an item can never be known."
21. REV LABEL HAZARDS in this file, for whoever works it next: "Mr. McBride" labels BOTH the prosecutor and the media-coalition attorney in adjacent turns (12:03–17:42); the oath at 01:14:17 is labeled to the witness "Caitlin Oliver" though content shows it being administered TO her; "Ms. Visser" at 02:08:12 covers an answer content shows is the witness's; victim's counsel appears as both "Jeff Dyer" and "Jeff Nyman" (cf. the profile's "Jeffrey Neiman"); "Judge" and "Judge Graf" alternate for the same speaker.

Neither document mentions immunity, a buccal swab, or the note being burned (0 hits, both readings) — the profile's §3.7/§4 tier markings for those rest entirely elsewhere.

Engraving text differs between the two same-day documents: Information "NoTices Bulge OWO What's This?" / "Hey Facist!" vs affidavit "Notices Buldge OWO what's this?" / "hey fascist!", against Robinson's text "notices bulge uwu" — the profile records none of this variance.

The Information says police "found a photograph of this note" without saying where or on whose device — the profile repeats the fact but not the silence, which bears on §11 open question 1.

**And it yields a fresh discrepancy no pass could have found from the Information alone:** the docket records **Charge 4 offense date 09-10-2025**; the Information pleads **COUNT 4 … "on or about September 11, 2025."** Exactly one row diverges (5 and 6 agree). This is the memory-index "metadata is not content" hazard live — an offence-date column is a typed field, not the pleading. **Queue: register the Count-4 date conflict; cite the pleading, never the docket column.**

### A4 — THE CONTINUANCE MOTION. Never opened; it dates the State's change of position.
`…/2026-04-02_motion-vacate-continue-march-27-2026_dc27998184.txt`

*"the State intends to present discrete 'buckets' of evidence through three identified law enforcement witnesses… and **testimony by Mr. Robinson's parents and roommate**."* ⚠️ **Recording layer — this is the defence's characterisation of the State's representations at a meeting, not a State filing.** But the sequence it opens is Tier-1 checkable and the profile has no trace of it: **2026-04-02 State plans roommate testimony → 2026-04-20 recorded 1102 statement taken → 2026-06-23 State opposes live testimony.** §6.2's flat *"The prosecution did not intend to call Twiggs live"* carries no date and no history.

### A5 — THE PRETRIAL PROTECTIVE ORDER. Never opened; answers a question §9 doesn't ask.
`…/2025-09-16_pretrial-protective-order_dkt14_dc27880872.txt` — the checked box is **"not an intimate partner or cohabitant, but is an alleged victim."** So dkt14's protected person is **not** Twiggs. §9 records an FBI protective detail at a T2/T3 boundary and never asks whether any *court* protection ever covered him. **Queue: state the null with its scope.**

### A6 — Opened by nobody, and genuinely empty (bank as negatives, don't re-run):
`oct24_clean.txt`, `tyler-robinson-ballistics-motion.txt`, `2025-09-12_public-safety-assessment-report_dkt3`, `2026-01-09_subpoena-sgt-david-hull_dkt291`, `2026-07-08_supplemental-notice-access-to-evidence`, `2025-12-15_ruling-clarifying-publicity-order_dkt242` — all zero on `Twigg|roommate|Luna|buccal|immunit`, both readings.

## (b) PROFILE CLAIMS NO PASS TESTED

### B1 — §4's statement of Mr. Twiggs' warned exposure is materially incomplete, and this is a living-persons item.
Profile: *"prosecutors warned Twiggs that knowingly making false statements could constitute a **Class A misdemeanor**"* (NewsNation, T3). The recording, played on day4 and now T1: *"Any false statement… may subject you to criminal punishment as a class A misdemeanor. **It potentially could be used for punishment as a high offense as obstruction of justice.**"* **He was warned of felony obstruction exposure as well, and the profile records only the misdemeanor.** Also T1 on the same recording: *"you've also been given a **federal agreement and a state agreement** granting you immunity."* And Davis's sworn definition of use immunity (day3, 01:04:24) corroborates §4's "not transactional" reading — **log the confirmation.**

### B2 — §7.1's "under oath" is T1, and the oath was administered **twice** because the recording failed.
day4: *"I realized my mic was muted at the onset… I'm going to repeat those admonitions to make sure we've got recording. So again, I placed you under oath."* **There is a segment at the head of the April-20 interview that was not captured.** Absent from §7.2's redaction/runtime accounting entirely, and it bears on the ≈37:00 arithmetic. Also: §7.1 says *"conducted by **a** prosecutor"* — content (not Rev labels) shows **at least two distinct questioners plus a third handling logistics**, in a room where the State separately recorded that Twiggs' counsel was present.

### B3 — §2.1's own "labeled inference" is answered by the declarant, and the profile says no source answers it.
Profile: *"(Labeled inference: the 'in person for the first time' phrasing… may imply prior online acquaintance; **no source confirms or dates any such contact**.)"* Twiggs, sworn, day4 (02:12:55): *"I think it was 2023. **I've known him longer than that though.** That was I think the first time I met him **in person**."* — and the prior connection is specified: *"I knew he knew **my friend group**."* **The "no source confirms" clause is now false against a file the library holds.**

### B4 — §3.1 hardens a hedge and converts an inference into an observation — the same defect §7.3 already has on the Dremel.
Profile: *"Robinson **left the apartment around 4–5 a.m.**"* Twiggs, sworn: *"**I didn't know he left early. I heard him leaving**… I don't know. I would've thought it was 5:00 AM because that's when an early work day was for him, but it was **probably more like 4:00**."* He did not see the departure and twice hedged the time. **Queue: restate §3.1 as an earwitness estimate with the hedge intact; this is a second instance of the §7.3 pattern, so it is a pattern finding, not an isolated slip.**

### B5 — §1.1's biography has no court-record footprint, and his own sworn occupation contradicts the gap.
`Utah Tech` = 0 and `Fossil` = 0 across day1–day5, both readings. His sworn answer: *"I was working. I was doing **bathtub and shower repair** and stuff and **new construction**."* §1.1 carries only "digital creator" and a Facebook-listed university.

### B6 — §5.2's central null was never tested by any pass. **I tested it; it holds.** Bank it.
Claim: *"the record does not contain any official allegation that Twiggs was present at UVU."* **Matching-capable pattern used:** co-occurrence of `Twiggs|roommate` within ±300 characters of `UVU|Utah Valley|campus|Orem|Losee|rooftop|the scene`, across day1–day5, the Information, the PC affidavit, the 2026-08-11 defence brief and the dkt289 disqualification opposition — **zero placements.** The four incidental co-occurrences are Robinson's own *"stuck in orem"* inside the thread, the affidavit's family-member paragraph, and the brief's identity discussion. **This is now a null with a stated scope and a pattern that could have matched.**

### B7 — §5's own heading is wrong, and §5's cast is incomplete.
Heading reads *"(Preliminary Hearing, Days 2–3)."* The Twiggs DNA record spans **Day 2 (FBI/Bakker) and Day 5 (ATF/Oliver)**. `Oliver` = 0 and `Caitlin` = 0 in the profile — **the examiner who put him on a third item is not named anywhere in the file.**

## (c) WHERE THE PROFILE'S SILENCE IS ITSELF A FINDING

### C1 — 🚨 STRONGEST ITEM. The denial was **without prejudice on stated conditions**, the conditions arrived, and nobody renewed. The profile never records the conditions and never asks.
Graf, 2026-06-23: *"this denial is **without prejudice and subject to renewal**… **If the State's presentation materially differs from the proffer** presented before the court, **or if defendant can identify specific testimony reasonably likely to negate probable cause, defendant may renew the request.**"*

The State then presented, 2026-07-06→10. **Across all five days and the 2026-08-11 defence brief: `quash` = 0, `certificate of attend` = 0, `77-21-3` = 0, `compulsory` = 0; `subpoena` appears twice on day4 and both are the *Discord* subpoena; the one `renew` on day5 is Novak renewing the electronic-media motion (docket 305).** Both readings. **The defence never renewed on this record.** And because 09-01 is argument on a closed evidentiary record, the window is effectively shut. §6.2 says only "quashed (without prejudice)"; §11 Q5 jumps straight to trial. **Queue: add the renewal condition to §6.2; add "was the renewal ever sought?" to §11 with the answer and its scope.**

### C2 — Graf expressly disclaimed the very thing §6's analytical note attributes to his ruling.
*"Nothing in this ruling should be understood as creating a **categorical prohibition** against subpoenas directed to non-victim witnesses."* / *"The court's ruling is **procedural rather than evidentiary**. Nothing in the decision… should be construed as a determination regarding the **credibility, reliability, weight, or ultimate admissibility** of any witness, statement, or item of evidence."* §6's note frames the ruling as letting confession evidence into the record through an unquestioned witness; the court said it decided nothing about that evidence.

### C3 — The legal mechanism by which Mr. Twiggs' statement entered the case is **entirely absent**. `1102` = 0 in the profile.
Graf: *"reliable hearsay, specifically a video recorded interview of Mr. Twiggs conducted pursuant to **Utah Rule of Evidence 1102**,"* under **Article I §12** and **Rule 7B**, against *Lopez / Schmidt / Ramirez*. The profile says only "Utah's preliminary-hearing hearsay allowance" and names one case. `Rule 1102`, `7B`, `14A`, `Schmidt`, `Ramirez` all = 0.

### C4 — §3.3 presents as one block two instructions the State pleaded on **two different days**, and the docket independently proves it.
Information: **COUNT 5** (`delete this exchange`) *"on or about **September 10**"*; **COUNT 6** (`ask for a lawyer and stay silent`) *"on or about **September 11**."* Docket charge table agrees. §3.3's "Robinson's closing instructions" runs them together — which matters because the defence moves to strike **Count Six only**, leaving Count Five, the count built on the instruction Mr. Twiggs disobeyed.

### C5 — Count 7 and the child. `child` = 0 in the profile.
The Information pleads a seventh count — *"criminal homicide in the physical presence of a **child younger than 14 years old**"* — and the defence brief contests it on the footage (*"None of the footage shows the individual identified as Mr. Robinson passing by or near individuals that are clearly minors"*). §10 resolves the charge count at "exactly seven" **without ever listing the seven**, so a reader cannot see that one is a Class A misdemeanour, cannot see the aggravating-factor NOTICE paragraphs that generate the "10 charges" arithmetic, and cannot see that the count list is contested.

### C6 — The strongest sworn support for §5.2's *prosecution* reading is on the record and the profile cites a press summary instead.
ATF examiner Caitlin Oliver, day5, on cross: *"It is **reasonable to assume that anyone living in the household could have contributed to DNA on an object found in that household**."* And Twiggs was one of **three** household elimination donors — *"Amber Robinson, Matthew Robinson, and Lance Twiggs… because you had information that those people were associated with the house."* §5.2 sources the secondary-transfer position to *"OANN summarizing the state's position."* **Per the objectivity rule, the side-supporting evidence must be logged as explicitly as the side-weakening evidence: the profile's §5 currently records neither of Robinson's parents as elimination donors, which is the fact that makes Mr. Twiggs' inclusion ordinary rather than singular.**

### C7 — File-convention conformance. The deliverable does not meet the project's own file standard.
`§0` = 0 (no four-tier framework reproduced), no §1 access-path caveat, **no File ID**, the file sits at repo root rather than in a `files - <Topic>` folder, and §13's revision log is prose, not the required `| Version | Date | Change |` table. Separately, the **Methodological Note's own four tiers diverge from the project standard**: its Tier 3 is *"named but non-official sources — relatives, friends, neighbors"*, which leaves **named journalism with no tier at all** — the mechanism by which press reporting has been silently absorbed throughout. And `CR-ACQ` = 0, `prelim-hearing-transcripts` = 0: **the profile contains not one reference to a held artifact path**, citing rev.com URLs for transcripts sitting on disk. That is the concrete form of the header's own warning that its tier markings are now "substantially false."
